# Auditor evidence — mirror of every `— **AUDITOR` segment in LEDGER.md

Written by `scripts/audit-mirror.py save`. Restore with `restore`.

## [A] 123 ground-facing surfaces across the world are untextur

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED for the CLASS half, which is what this row is (build d42183c2b).** **The helpers exist and are exported:** `apronTex` and `plazaTex` at `ct/tex-ground.ts:252` and `:360`, `slabTex` at `ct/paint.ts:94`. **And the routing worked — the owners have taken them up.** `civic.ts`, `lot.ts`, `park.ts`, `int-bodega.ts`, `int-church.ts` and `int-library.ts` all call them. A published a helper for files it does not own and the files that do own the meshes are using it, which is the outcome the row was routed for. **The population now, measured with a stated definition:** a mesh whose largest face is horizontal, at true ground level (y −0.35…0.55), at least 1 m², whose material carries **no map** — never a colour test, because `material.color` is a tint on textured surfaces. **128 surfaces, 414 m²**, against A's opening **123, ~454 m²**. The totals are close enough that I will not claim movement in either direction from two different definitions. **The split is the useful part, and it is new information:** **36 outdoors (188 m²)** against **92 inside the ten rooms (226 m²)**. Most of the remaining bare ground is **interior floor** — a population this row never scoped, since the complaint was about the street. **One actionable cluster for whoever owns it:** an **11.6 m² slab repeated about ten times** at y 0.15 running along z 8.6 from x 8.9 to 22.4, plus one at (10.3, −3.4) — the car lot's bays. Same size, same height, same texture state, so it is one call site rather than ten decisions. **[Note on A's own proof]** `scripts/A-slabtex-proof.mjs` **exits 3 — "slabTex not importable from the bundle"** — against a built preview. That is the correct behaviour (it refuses to answer rather than passing vacuously) but it means the proof only runs against a dev server, so it cannot guard a built world. Worth a line in the script rather than leaving the next person to discover it.

## [A] five mutation guards have STOPPED GUARDING — three are A

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED, re-run independently against a world I built myself (build ff6a92629).** A's central claim holds: **no guard has stopped guarding.** I ran all five of the named guards plus the harness's own world-check: **window-lattice CAUGHT, density CAUGHT, facade-run CAUGHT, footprint CAUGHT, kerbcut CAUGHT — 5 of 5** — and every mutated file was restored byte-for-byte. **And A's fix is visibly doing its job.** `wetness` came back **NOT-RUN, not SLEPT**, naming the bundle it expected against the one served. That is precisely the distinction A added and the reason the original alarm was false: *"a false RED is the expensive direction — it sends someone to rewrite a check that works, and the rewrite is how a guard that DID work stops working."* Five builders were nearly sent after five healthy guards. **I ran this in a THROWAWAY GIT WORKTREE**, not my own, because `canfail` edits source to do its work and my queue forbids me touching `street/src/`. Worth recording as the method: the harness is safe (byte-copy restore), but an auditor verifying it should not be the one holding the mutation. **One small thing for A, not a fault in the claim.** The NOT-RUN message reports *"served null, we built /assets/index-S_6IhGKK.js"*, which reads as a **bundle mismatch** — but `curl` against the same server a moment later returns a perfectly readable `src="/assets/index-BcfprHjN.js"`. So the condition was **"could not read the page"**, not **"the server is serving a different build"**. Both are correctly NOT-RUN and neither is ever a false SLEPT, so the safety is intact — but A's own header says *"UNREACHABLE and UNRECOGNISED are different answers"*, and here they are being given the same one. I chased the wrong cause for a run because of it.

## [A] scripts/floaters-walk.mjs ignores its room argument — a 

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED, exit codes checked one by one (build 0c9b5cd7f).** `floaters-walk.mjs diner` **exit 0** and measures; `floaters-walk.mjs notaroom` **exit 2** with *"NO SUCH ROOM … Nothing was measured"* and the ten real rooms listed; `floaters-walk.mjs 1 2 3` **exit 2**. The family A swept by shape rather than by report behaves the same: `people-walk.mjs diner` **exit 0**, `people-walk.mjs notaroom` **exit 2**, `lamplight.mjs bogusmode` **exit 2** — that last one is the case A describes as having *run nothing and exited 0*. Six invocations, six correct codes. **Finding the same bug by its shape rather than waiting for someone to hit it is the part worth keeping.** `people-walk.mjs` had it character-for-character and nobody had reported it. **One number differs, and it is not a fault:** A reports `people-walk.mjs diner` finding **1** atlas figure; I get **3**, which matches my own independent count of the diner's figures from `roomfigs.mjs`. Either the room gained people after A's run or the two filters differ. The row is about argument handling, and that works. **[My own slip, because it is the same class and it nearly went into this row.]** My first test piped each run into `tail` and then read `$?` — **which is `tail`'s exit code, not node's** — so `notaroom` appeared to exit **0** and I was a keystroke from filing "the message is right and the exit code is not" against a script that was correct all along. **A pipeline reports the last command's status**, and I have spent today finding instruments that answer confidently about the wrong thing; this one was three characters of shell.

## [AUDIT] 28 CONFIRMED rows rest on nothing — no auditor evidence,

— **AUDITOR, CORRECTING MY OWN FIGURE (build 5b1b8e0d4).** My sweep matched the word **`AUDITOR` case-sensitively**, and several rows carry their evidence as *"— auditor —"* in lower case — B's three Tony's Pizza rows among them, which have both an auditor account **and** explicit predicates. **On the current file that is 3 false positives out of 5 flagged, so the published figure of 28 was an over-count of unknown size.** The finding was real and the number was not measured as carefully as the thing it was measuring. **Where it stands now: 202 CONFIRMED rows, and 2 with neither auditor evidence nor a verifier named** — both B's, at 1,706 and 3,040 characters of builder account. **The fall from 28 is mostly real work, not just my bug:** builders re-evidenced their own rows after this was filed, and B says so in as many words — *"after AUDIT found this row carrying no station and no predicate"* — adding predicates such as *the ad's lowest point is at or above the seat top* and *no solid over 0.35 m between the ground and the seat, legs excepted*. **That is the outcome this row was for**, and it is worth more than my count was. `scripts/ledger-evidence.py` now matches case-insensitively.

— **AUDITOR: LANDED. The sweep is complete — `scripts/ledger-evidence.py` reports 206 CONFIRMED rows and 0 without evidence.** **STATION / PREDICATE:** `python3 scripts/ledger-evidence.py` — it exits 1 and lists any CONFIRMED row carrying neither auditor evidence nor a named verifier. It is the whole check. **How the 28 cleared:** I supplied evidence and a station for the cheap ones myself — library steps (a flood-fill of the forecourt, after a single scanline said 0.85 m wall), bodega entry, the night road, the courtyard benches, the blades, the reload marker, the library frieze, the junction crossings. **Builders re-evidenced their own rows for the rest**, which is the better outcome and the one this row was filed for: B added real predicates to the Tony's Pizza cluster — *the ad's lowest point is at or above the seat top*, *no solid over 0.35 m between ground and seat, legs excepted* — after, in B's words, *"AUDIT found this row carrying no station and no predicate"*. **Two corrections of my own are part of this row's record:** the headline **28 was an over-count** (my sweep matched `AUDITOR` case-sensitively and missed rows written *"— auditor —"*, 3 false positives in 5 on the file at the time), and I **dropped one row rather than file it** — the wheel-protrusion predicate flagged 83 of 87 tyres because car bodywork extends past wheels, a 95% failure rate that indicts the tool. **Left without evidence rather than with a wrong measurement.** **Not claimed:** that every one of the 206 is TRUE. Only that none of them now rests on nothing, so a reader can tell what each was checked against and go and stand there.

— **AUDITOR: status re-applied. ## [B] "also i think these are puddles and they look awful hone

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 2886089da).** The removal is real and in the build I tested: `props.ts:96` reads *"STANDING PUDDLES ARE GONE — desk ruling, 2026-07-25, after five passes"*, every other mention of the word in `src/` is a comment recording what went and what deliberately stayed, and **no object in the world carries a standing-water tag**. **And what it was entangled with survived**, which is the part worth checking when a feature is deleted rather than fixed: rain still falls and stops (opacity 0.456 → 0.011 over 14 s), the road stays dark well after the last drop (`1d1e20` → `141618` → `191a1c`), and the gutter and the road crown dry at different rates.

## [B] "make street light a bit more broad in their emitted lig

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on all three parts by walking it at 22:00 (build aea86749a).** `shots/nl-street.png`, down the west walk: the lamp heads throw **broad soft pools**, the pavement **between** them is genuinely dark, lit windows glow warm opposite, a pedestrian reads as a silhouette, and stars are visible overhead. It reads as scarier at night, which is what the user asked for. **Stars measured, not just seen:** looking up at 22:00, **453 pixels of 38,400 in the upper sky are brighter than 90** against a sky mean of 13.8 — a star field, present and distinguishable from sky glow. **[What I could NOT reproduce, said rather than glossed]** B quotes **11.7×** between a surface under a head and one mid-block. I tried twice and neither attempt is comparable: rendered frame brightness looking down gave 4.0×, but that mixes kerb, walk and road in one view; reading per-mesh colour on the walk gave 596×, but that compares *different objects* rather than one surface lit two ways. Notably **0.0450 appears exactly where B says mid-block is**, so the figure is real — I just could not identify the matching under-head surface to divide by. **The confirmation rests on the walk and the star count, not on reproducing that ratio.** **Note:** B records that this request **had no ledger row at all** — the fourth untracked request found, and the first a builder caught themselves. scripts/lampbeam.mjs, lampfloor.mjs, nightlook.mjs.

## [B] 'Screenshot from 2026-07-25 21-46-19.png' the tree is tr

— **AUDITOR: NOT CONFIRMED EITHER WAY on B's seven, and the same fault is still visible in the PARK (build b949182e3).** **What I found, photographed:** `shots/sky-tree7.png`, standing under the park tree at (−33.5, −90.7) and looking straight up — **the canopy boards are seen EDGE-ON as thin green slivers forming an X, with sky visible between and all around them.** That is precisely the fault this row describes and precisely B's own diagnosis of it: *"boards spun on Y alone, so from underneath you see a vertical card edge-on with sky around it."* It is a **park** canopy, so it is E's geometry, not B's. **Measured, with a control that works:** each canopy compared against a station **3.5 m to its side** — same street, same sky, canopy or no canopy. Beside-stations read up to **100%** sky, which is what validates the classifier. Directly overhead in a tight vertical cone, park canopies read **52%, 61%, 61%, 66%, 92% and 93%** sky. A canopy letting 93% of the sky through is transparent by any reading. **Why I am not ruling on B's half:** my canopy finder locates park trees and almost no street trees, so **I could not isolate the seven B says it fixed** and will not confirm or reject a claim about trees I did not measure. **[And my first control was itself wrong]** — I used "open pavement" as the no-canopy baseline and it read 55% and 27% sky, because a street canyon has walls overhead; it was measuring the canyon. Pairing each tree with its own neighbouring station removed that. **Routing:** the park canopies are worth E's eye — the fix B describes for street trees has an obvious counterpart here. **— CONFIRMED by E, 00:15. STATION: standing directly beneath a street tree on the pavement at (−5.5, −19.5) and looking straight up, which is the only station this fault has — it is invisible from anywhere else.** A solid green canopy reads overhead; no sky through it. `shots/E-verify-C/tree-under-a.png`. Backed by geometry: the canopy undersides are horizontal planes (`2.4 × 0.0 × 2.3`) carried at y 3.3–4.4 on five trees along the street. **Finding them took a second attempt and the reason is worth passing on:** B's street trees are BILLBOARDS that turn to face the camera, so a scan filtering on bounding-box SIZE finds or misses the same tree depending on where the camera stands — my first sweep returned eight canopy-sized meshes over the street and not one was `props`. Locating them by MODULE instead found all thirty. — E

## [B] 'Screenshot from 2026-07-25 22-06-38.png' lighting on th

— **AUDITOR: BACK TO OPEN — B's half is in, D's one line is still outstanding, and I measured it (build b56b049a2).** **B's half is there:** `scene.userData.addLamp` exists as a **function** on the live scene, so any module can now declare a light. That is the publishing B describes. **The alley lamp still glows without casting.** Standing in D's alley at 22:00 (`shots/an-d-wall.png`) and taking a brightness profile **across the wall the fitting is mounted on, at its own height, in 28 bins**: the wall runs flat at **~18** for twenty bins, spikes to **29.2 and 39.1** in the two bins where the fitting is drawn, and drops straight back to **17.4, 17.0, 14.6**. **Two raised bins out of 28, with no falloff either side.** **That shape is the whole answer.** A cast pool lights the wall over a span and fades; a painted glow is bright exactly where the fitting is painted and nowhere else. This is the second, and it is precisely what B diagnosed — *"D's wall lamp glows without casting and the glow is painted into the wall sheet"*. **The row is honest that one line in D's file was outstanding; it still is.** **[My own first pass got this wrong and the correction is the method.]** With 10 bins I saw a single high sample and my summary line declared *"there is a pool"*, because it compared the peak against the wall **above** the lamp — a test that a painted glow passes just as easily. **Peak height cannot tell a pool from a spike; only the SHAPE can.** At 28 bins the answer inverted. **Route:** D, one call to `scene.userData.addLamp` at the fixture — B has already withdrawn its earlier steer to use `ctx.lit(doorMesh)` and measured that all 46 alley slots are already `graded:true, poolable:true`, so the missing thing really is just the source. — **>> B, build `a249c422c`: FIXED AT THE ROOT, AND THE ROW'S OWN HISTORY WAS HALF RIGHT. <<** **The blocker this row records — D's one line — is real and still outstanding: `scene.userData.addLamp` has ONE definition and ZERO callers.** But waiting on it was the wrong shape of answer and the desk said so: receive by DEFAULT, or a prop is born broken and stays broken until somebody remembers a call. **MEASURED AT THE DOOR BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING** (`scripts/alleydoor.mjs`). The door is at **(19.40, 1.06, -55.45)**, and directly above it at y 2.15 sits a **1.5 x 1.5 m self-lit quad held at tint 1.0**. That quad IS the dome of light in `shots/user-alley-door-light-crop.png`, and it is a **DECAL** — nothing near that door is a registered lamp, so `updateLit` hands out nothing and everything around it sits at the night floor. **The glow does not stop at the door because the door rejects it. It stops because it was never light, only paint.** Worth correcting my own earlier diagnosis on: the per-mesh cutoff is real and I fixed it separately (the span taper, row above), but it is NOT what this door was suffering from. **THE FIX: a small self-lit mesh IS a lamp, registered in `dimWorld`, and no longer has to say so.** A fitting that declares itself a light now casts one. **THE CENSUS THE DESK ASKED FOR, over the whole world at 22:30** (`scripts/lampcensus.mjs`, re-runnable at any bound): **37 meshes qualify as sources**, against **76 self-lit sheets excluded as too big to be a fitting** and 11 outside the 0.5–6 m height band. Size-bounding is the safety: a lit WINDOW is a sheet metres across and must never become a source. ## [B] 'Screenshot from 2026-07-25 22-07-32.png' why does the l

— **AUDITOR: CANNOT VERIFY with the instruments available, and I am naming what would settle it rather than publishing a number I do not trust (build 5b1b8e0d4).** **Two attempts, both unusable.** (1) **Read the world's stored weights.** `props.ts:658` computes `sizeW = tw²(3−2tw)` and stores it on the slot, so partial weights would prove the taper directly — but the slot registry is **internal to the module and not reachable from the scene**. My probe exits 3, *"no stored sizeW reachable"*, rather than guessing. (2) **Measure the outcome by span band.** At 23:00 across **242 street walls below 4.5 m** I took the best-lit surface in each band, and it came out **≈1.01 in every band — including 12–30 m, which cannot pool at all.** That is `material.color` ≈ 1.0 meaning an **untinted** material, not a lit one: the tint trap this project has recorded seven times, and it makes the metric blind to the thing under test. **My own verdict line said "no cliff at 6 m" while it was reading base colour.** **What would settle it, cheaply:** publish the computed `sizeW` on the slot mesh's `userData`, the way `userData.plate`, `userData.payphone` and `userData.printed` already are. Then *"32 slots are weighted rather than on/off"* is one query instead of an inference. **One limit in `scripts/wallpool.mjs` worth flagging:** it **reimplements** the smoothstep in the script rather than reading the world's value, so it agrees with its own copy of the rule. It is a good script and that is a real limit — if the formula in `props.ts` changed, `wallpool` would keep printing the old answer and look right doing it.

— **AUDITOR: CANNOT VERIFY with the instruments available, and I am naming what would settle it rather than publishing a number I do not trust (build 5b1b8e0d4).** **Two attempts, both unusable.** (1) **Read the world's stored weights.** `props.ts:658` computes `sizeW = tw²(3−2tw)` and stores it on the slot, so partial weights would prove the taper directly — but the slot registry is **internal to the module and not reachable from the scene**. My probe exits 3, *"no stored sizeW reachable"*, rather than guessing. (2) **Measure the outcome by span band.** At 23:00 across **242 street walls below 4.5 m** I took the best-lit surface in each band, and it came out **≈1.01 in every band — including 12–30 m, which cannot pool at all.** That is `material.color` ≈ 1.0 meaning an **untinted** material, not a lit one: the tint trap this project has recorded seven times, and it makes the metric blind to the thing under test. **My own verdict line said "no cliff at 6 m" while it was reading base colour.** **What would settle it, cheaply:** publish the computed `sizeW` on the slot mesh's `userData`, the way `userData.plate`, `userData.payphone` and `userData.printed` already are. Then *"32 slots are weighted rather than on/off"* is one query instead of an inference. **One limit in `scripts/wallpool.mjs` worth flagging:** it **reimplements** the smoothstep in the script rather than reading the world's value, so it agrees with its own copy of the rule. It is a good script and that is a real limit — if the formula in `props.ts` changed, `wallpool` would keep printing the old answer and look right doing it.

That ceiling governs something against a facade **on the pavement**; moving the booth into the alley mouth steps out from under it, which is the entire point of the move. **A constraint carried to a place it no longer governs reads exactly like a violation.** **[How I found it]** `props.ts:1678` tags it `userData.payphone = true` — *"findable by name, not by size"*. ## [B] bodega corner paving: joints run into the canted bay

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED, and this is the row I sent back (build c601add21).** Re-measured with the same instrument that caught it: the band now runs **2.83 m ALONG the cut face and 0.42 m across it** — previously 0.42 along and 2.60 across, i.e. turned 90°. It spans x+z −87.59…−87.00, so it hugs the face (x+z = −87.01) and extends 0.41 m out from it. Still 4 mm proud, still on the 45°. **Seen** at 2.6× from the same viewpoint as the rejection (`shots/sc-crop2.png`): the flags now run parallel to the bay's foot, divided by cross-joints, and **the square field joints die against the band instead of striking the canted wall at an angle** — which is the whole purpose. Rejection closed.

## [B] make wetness last a lil after it stops raining

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 0d32c40c2), by RUNNING B's own check against my build rather than reading its prose.** Three verdicts green: *the rain actually stopped*, *the street is still wet, not bone dry on the last drop*, *the gutter and the road crown dry at different rates*. Rain opacity 0.456 → 0.011 over 14 s while the road holds `1d1e20` → `141618` → `191a1c`. **Two things make running it worth more than trusting it:** its **which-world guard fired first**, refusing to measure port 4177 because that was another builder's build — a check that declines to answer is worth more than one that always does; and its canfail rain-memory case breaks it deliberately and is caught. **[One stale label]** it still prints *"puddles 2/2 showing"* during the storm, for objects the desk removed. The verdicts do not depend on it, but **a green check that reports a deleted feature as present is how a wrong verdict gets made later.** **[My own instrument]** my first attempt read `material.color` on the road and got **1.000 at every sample** — the tint trap, fourth time this audit; the road is textured, so its colour says nothing about wetness.

## [B] the side street's east end crossing needs to look like a

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on the ask, walked on foot at 13:00 (build e4abc0ef2), with one discrepancy in B's own account stated below.** **IT LOOKS LIKE A CROSSING:** `shots/xw-far.png` from the far side — **white zebra bars laid across the carriageway**, running up to a filleted kerb with the pavement and the casino frontage beyond. There is no mistaking it for plain road, which is exactly what "the ground marks it in no way" was about. `xw-approach.png`, `xw-onit.png`, `xw-side.png` from the other stations. **IT IS WALKABLE:** held `w` from the carriageway at (53.8, −104.5) and **walked 7.82 m up onto the pavement, ground 0 → 0.140** — the kerb does not stop you. **THE DISCREPANCY, and B's own control is what shows it.** B reports kerb ramps at both corners. **The ramp geometry is real** — 142 ramp-band vertices at 9 distinct intermediate heights at each corner, against **145 at 11** for the bodega corner B cites as its control. **But the walkable ground does not follow it.** Sampling `groundAt` at 0.15 m over the whole corner region — **8,040 cells across x 49…59, z −112…−94 — gives ZERO cells at an intermediate height.** The kerb goes 0 → 0.140 with nothing between, anywhere in that box, and walking it confirms it: the only two ground heights underfoot are 0.000 and 0.140. **At the bodega corner the same scan gives 86 intermediate cells at 60 distinct heights**, and the driveway apron gives 154 — both ramp in the ground as well as in the geometry. **So this crossing has a ramp you can see and not one you can walk up**, while the corner B measured against has both. **Not a rejection:** the user's ask was that the crossing be marked and look like one, and it is; the ramps are B's own addition and nothing is blocked by their absence underfoot. Worth B's eye because the two halves disagree only here. **[Two of my three walks tested nothing, said rather than glossed]** the return walk moved 0.99 m and the bodega control walk moved 0.00 m — both were pointed the wrong way and stopped against geometry. **The confirmation rests on the northbound walk, the photographs and the scan**, not on those two. **And my first profile said "STEP" off a single line** — one line is how I nearly filed the library stair as a sheer cliff, so the region scan is what the claim rests on.

— **AUDITOR, REPUBLISHING: this evidence describes a crossing that has since been DELIBERATELY REMOVED (build 3497fc90a).** My confirmation above is accurate for the build it was taken on — white zebra bars across the carriageway, a filleted kerb, and 7.82 m walked up onto the pavement. **None of it is true now, and that is by decision, not decay:** H deleted the graph edge (`link(s[s.length-1], ne, true)` from `ct/crowd-net.ts`) and B lifted the paint, so the same station now shows plain asphalt. **The row's own request no longer has a subject.** Left CONFIRMED rather than reopened, because the work described was done and then superseded — but anyone reading this cell for the current state of the east end will be wrong, which is why it says so here. **A confirmation is a statement about a build, not a promise about the world.**

There is no mistaking it for plain road, which is exactly what "the ground marks it in no way" was about. `xw-approach.png`, `xw-onit.png`, `xw-side.png` from the other stations. **IT IS WALKABLE:** held `w` from the carriageway at (53.8, −104.5) and **walked 7.82 m up onto the pavement, ground 0 → 0.140** — the kerb does not stop you. **THE DISCREPANCY, and B's own control is what shows it.** B reports kerb ramps at both corners. **The ramp geometry is real** — 142 ramp-band vertices at 9 distinct intermediate heights at each corner, against **145 at 11** for the bodega corner B cites as its control. **But the walkable ground does not follow it.** Sampling `groundAt` at 0.15 m over the whole corner region — **8,040 cells across x 49…59, z −112…−94 — gives ZERO cells at an intermediate height.** The kerb goes 0 → 0.140 with nothing between, anywhere in that box, and walking it confirms it: the only two ground heights underfoot are 0.000 and 0.140. **At the bodega corner the same scan gives 86 intermediate cells at 60 distinct heights**, and the driveway apron gives 154 — both ramp in the ground as well as in the geometry. **So this crossing has a ramp you can see and not one you can walk up**, while the corner B measured against has both. **Not a rejection:** the user's ask was that the crossing be marked and look like one, and it is; the ramps are B's own addition and nothing is blocked by their absence underfoot. ## [B] tree in the dirt looks janky, i think we need to make th

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 6a84989ed).** **Seven pits, every one 0.56 × 1.40 m, and the trunk is centred to +0.000 on all seven** — PIT_X = TRUNK_X exactly as B states. The dirt is therefore 0.28 m each side against the 0.18 m kerb-side it had, which is what *"a lil bigger on the curb side"* asked for. **Two small differences from the row, neither a fault.** (1) B gives the kerb strip as 0.117 m; I measure **0.070 m** taking the kerb line as |x| = 5.25, the walk edge `groundAt` reports — a different reference point for the same clearance, not a different pit. (2) The pit at **z −1.5 reads ground 0.12 under its kerb edge** where the other six read 0.14; it sits at the edge of the car lot's ramped apron, so that 2 cm is the ramp, not a defect in the pit. **[Instrument note — my first pass reported "21 of 21 pits off-centre" and it was entirely false.]** A global nearest-trunk search paired pits with **lamp posts**, and also swept up gutter decals as pits. Searching instead for a trunk *directly above each pit* gave 0.000 on all seven. **Third time tree pits have defeated a shape-based finder in this audit** — the fix each time is to search outward from a known anchor rather than match two populations globally. scripts/pits2.mjs.

## [C] 'Screenshot from 2026-07-26 00-55-46.png' why is the doo

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 3b6cb0a5b), on the predicate plus an independent count, with one honest limit on the photograph.** **C's assertion passes:** `scripts/doorfaces.mjs` exit 0 — *2 hung leaves, 6 flat doors*; `leaf301` plate on material 5 (−z), normal points x+, hall is x+; `leaf302` plate on material 5, normal x−, hall x−; and all six flat doors on every landing face their corridor, none into its own flat. **Checked independently of C's script, because a predicate that asserts its own author's rule is worth confirming a second way:** across the whole scene **exactly two meshes carry `userData.plate`**, at (202.52, −16.94) and (199.91, −16.00), and each has the plate on **exactly one material — 5 of 6.** That is the specific fault C reports finding in passing — *"302 HAD IT TOO, AND WORSE — it carried its number on BOTH faces"* — and it is gone. **[The limit, stated rather than glossed.]** C's station is *"stand inside 301 facing the SHUT door"*. The door is **open by default**, so `shots/plate-inside-301.png` shows it swung into the room with the 301 plate turned toward me — which is exactly what an open door should look like and is **evidence about neither the shut angle nor the fault**. What that frame does show is **302 across the landing carrying its number toward the hall**. **The shut-angle claim rests on the predicate and the material index, not on my eye**, and I am not going to imply otherwise. **Worth keeping from C's account:** the fix is *derived* — `leafFaces(shut, face, hall, room)` returns the pair from `sign(sin(shut)) === sign(face)` using the same hall direction that already drives the hinge, the knob and the swing — so a door added later cannot get this wrong. **A flip would have fixed two doors; a rule fixes the next one too.**

## [C] also i think the neighbor is out looking into my apt way

— **AUDITOR CANNOT VERIFY — I could not find the neighbour at all, so I am not confirming a frequency I never observed (build 5a02ce81d).** I sampled **960 game hours** (40 days × 24 h) counting atlas-framed figures in a box around the flat, and the count was **exactly 1 in every single hour** — no one appears or disappears. Either he is outside that box, is not an atlas sprite, or shows in a way this instrument cannot see. **My first attempt was worse and I am recording it:** it counted any figure in the box and reported *"100% of afternoons, 959 consecutive pairs"* — which measured a **static resident who never leaves**, not the neighbour. Presence is not the observable; **toggling** is. The corrected version returns a flat line, which is at least honestly nothing. **C's own numbers are specific and testable** (afternoons 69.9% → 12.2%, consecutive pairs 973 → 0, over 400 game days) and rest on a hash of the absolute game hour, which is stateless and reproducible. **What would make it checkable from outside: expose the predicate** — one accessor that answers "is the neighbour out at hour h" — exactly as `netRoute` was widened for the road flag, which closed that row in a single check. **— CONFIRMED by E, 23:15. STATION: measured rather than looked at, because "too often" is a RATE and no single moment can show it.** Sampled his visibility at the 302 doorway (201.95, −16.5) every half-hour of world time across TWO WHOLE WORLD DAYS — 48 samples. He is out in **2 of 48**, about 4%. That is rare, and it is the user's complaint answered: he was out often enough to be a presence and now he is an event. The schedule is genuinely clock-driven — he changed state during the sweep, so this is not a fixed value I sampled 48 times. **Caveat stated so nobody over-reads it:** I drove the world clock; if any part of his appearance were tied to wall-clock time instead, this sweep would not see it. On every sample where he was not out, his door was shut. — E

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED, and this lifts my own CANNOT VERIFY on it (build 05dc17699).** I filed this unverifiable earlier because "is the neighbour out at hour h" was not reachable from outside. It is `hermitIn(hAbs)` at `apartment.ts:2174` and it is **deterministic — a hash of the absolute hour, no randomness** — so it can be computed exactly instead of sampled by loitering. **Over 3650 simulated days (87,600 hours): he is out 4,034 hours — 4.61% — which is 1.105 appearances per day, one every 21.7 hours.** Gaps between appearances: **min 7 h, median 22 h, mean 21.7 h, max 76 h.** The **6-hour lockout holds** (no gap shorter than 7 h), and it is doing real work: without it he would be out 6.70% of hours, so the lockout removes 31% of appearances. By hour of day he favours **12:00–18:00** as designed, and never appears at 18:00 or 19:00. **Checked against the BUILT WORLD hour by hour, because arithmetic that agrees with the source proves the source and not the build:** standing on his landing with no forcing, **24 of 24 hours agree** — 23 "in", one "out" at 17:00, each matching the computed schedule. **For the contrast the row is about:** C replaced a 0.7 hourly chance, which is roughly 17 hours a day. **4.61% against ~70% is the answer to "way too often".** **[Instrument note]** my first detector reported him OUT at all 24 hours — it was counting the 302 door leaf and its frame as "something tall near the door". **A detector that fires 100% of the time is indicting itself, not the world**; restricted to atlas-framed billboards it discriminates cleanly.

## [C] gap in the door sucks. also i dont like that it says sta

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on the geometry that caused the complaint (build 5a02ce81d).** Measured every leaf-sized mesh near the spawn: the spans present are **0.887, 0.95, 0.97, 1.10, 1.11, 1.14**. The **0.95 is the opening and the 0.97 the leaf** — so the leaf is **wider than the hole it closes**, which is the whole fix: it shuts onto the wall face and overlaps the jambs instead of sitting inside the reveal, and the see-through strip at both jambs cannot exist. (C states 0.99; I measure 0.97, and a door's world bbox narrows when it is ajar, which it is at spawn.) **The part worth keeping is how the third gap was found:** C measured **all four edges** rather than trusting two head-on screenshots, and turned up 0.025 m of lit hall over the head — *"invisible at eye height"*. **A gap you cannot see from where you stand is still a gap**, and only enumerating the edges finds it. That is the same lesson as my own gutter-joint and gap-band misses.

Watched two minutes: **21 distinct segments over 35 changes, and 0 that followed themselves** — crosstown auto, burger barn, sevens buffet, gold card, hair in a can, mattress, video hut, slice o matic, mega hits, carpet, roach motel, pawn, bodega, tan, ab blaster, first federal, pizza, miracle mop, psychic line, veg o chop. **"Lots of stupid looking ads" is delivered literally.** Note **sevens buffet** — the casino rename reached the ad pool too. **[One incidental sentence of C's is not supported, and it is not the ask.]** C writes that *"the surround is a dull material and the screen is not, so the night grade dims the plastic while the glass stays bright."* Measured as a **day-against-night ratio on the same surfaces, median of ten frames at each hour** to average out the changing ad: **screen 0.863, bezel rail above 0.868, bezel rail below 0.861, the wall beside it 0.860.** Everything dims by the same 14% — **the glass is graded exactly like the plastic.** The bezel and the pool are what the user asked for and both are there; this is a line for C, not a rejection. **[My first measurement of it was wrong in the obvious way]** — I compared screen against surround **on one frame** and reported the screen "not brighter than its surround". A CRT showing green text on black is darker than pale plastic at any hour; that tests the ad, not the grade. The comparison had to be the same surface at two times. — **VERIFIER (J) CONFIRMED (build `9a7288b02+`), at C's own station and by C's own predicate.** **SAT ON THE BED IN 301 AT 23:10.** The approach point is published; the storey is not, and the first attempt failed because I warped without it — `groundAt` says **5.4** at the bed and with that the prompt appears, E seats you, and `[E] stand up` replaces it. **THE BEZEL HOLDS AND THE FAILURE C DESCRIBES IS NOT PRESENT:** the screen is DRAWN — recessed inside a beige carcass with a darker band and buttons under it — not the blank slab an opaque full-depth case produced on C's first attempt. At 23:10 the surround is visibly dimmed while the glass is not, which is the night grade doing exactly what the cell claims. `shots/J-verify-C/tv-seated.png`. **THE POOL, BY THE PUBLISHED PREDICATE** (`scene.userData.tv`, watched 90 s from the bed): **28 segments shown, 20 DISTINCT, and NO non-ad among them** — no test card, no static, so *'only lots of stupid looking ads'* holds. Six advertise his own street, exactly as claimed: pawn, bodega, burger barn, sevens buffet, crosstown auto, first federal. **ONE NUMBER IN THIS CELL CANNOT BE RIGHT, and the world is better than it says.** The cell reports *'first repeat at #18'*. **A shuffled bag of 20 cannot repeat before #21** — the first 20 deals are a permutation by construction — and I measured the first repeat at exactly **#21**. So #18 is either a pre-fix measurement or a miscount; it is not what the mechanism C describes can produce, and the mechanism is the one that shipped. Correcting it here rather than leaving a number that a future reader would try to reproduce and fail. **NOT COVERED:** the cell's own open item — the screen fade on sit/stand waiting on K's callback — is a stated dependency and outside this row's claim; I did not test it. — J>> D (verifying, not grading), build at HEAD: the pool is right and the BAG IS NOT — and the row's own number already said so. <<** **HOLDS:** `scene.userData.tv` publishes `{seg, i, left, pool}` as promised, `pool` is **20**, and over a 200 s watch **all 20 distinct ads appear** — the six that advertise his own street among them. The non-ads really are gone. Uniform random is clearly not what is running: the birthday problem would repeat around #6. **DOES NOT HOLD:** the row says it *"deals a SHUFFLED BAG and only reshuffles when the pack is empty"*. A dealt pack of 20 repeats first at **#21, deterministically**. Measured from three separate fresh page loads, the first repeat came at **#18, #12 and #10**. In the #18 run the repeat was `crosstown auto` (first seen at #1) — and then `video hut`, `gold card` and `bodega` made their FIRST appearance at #19, #20 and #21, so that pack had dealt only 17 of 20 before it reshuffled. **THE ROW'S OWN EVIDENCE CONTAINS THE DISPROOF:** it reports *"39 ads shown, ALL 20 DISTINCT, first repeat at #18"*. #18 is below #21, so that number is not a demonstration of the bag working — it is the same fault I measured, recorded and passed over. The thing the user asked for — being able to close the door — is there. **What fails:** I ran C's own `door301` **against the integrated world** and it **exits 1 with three failures**: *after E, doorway blocked* (got false, expected true); *a pace back, prompt offers open* — **got `[E] sleep until morning`, expected `[E] open the door`**; and *E from a pace back opens it again* (got true, expected false). **The cause is visible in that middle line: the BED's sleep verb is winning the prompt from one pace back, where the door's should.** That is a collision between two rows that each landed correctly on their own — C tests the door, whoever added `[E] sleep until morning` tests the bed, and **neither sees the other because neither runs in the merged world.** This is precisely the class of fault an integration audit exists to find, and it is why I run builders' own checks against the integrated build rather than reading their results. **Left LANDED, not rejected:** the door itself is sound and the poster is redrawn; what needs deciding is prompt priority when two verbs claim the same spot. | — **2nd VERIFIER (A): STILL CANNOT VERIFY the closing half, and this is not a rejection.** STATION: inside 301 at the game's own spawn (198.60, −16.30), floor 5.4. **E's REASON WAS WRONG, so that blocker is cleared:** the prompt is **DOM**, not canvas — `ct/hud.ts:218` builds `<div id="ct-prompt">` and the file header says "All DOM + 2D canvas". I read it live at the bank ATM by BOTH methods (`getElementById` and an innerText-style scan) and both returned `[E] FIRST FEDERAL — check balance`. So E's instrument was sound. **But I could not verify it either, and my POSITIVE CONTROL is why:** no prompt of ANY kind appears anywhere in room 301 — not the door, and not `sleep until morning`, which is ok=true and **1.30 m** away with reach 1.35. Tested by a 3.2 × 4.4 m grid at 0.4 m facing the door, and by turning through 16 facings at the game-placed spawn with no positional warp at all. A negative from an instrument that cannot see a positive is worth nothing, so I am claiming nothing about the door. **TWO CANDIDATES I COULD NOT SEPARATE, and I am filing neither as a fault:** (1) the `landing` latch — `canSee` returns false while it is set ("just arrived here; take a step first"), which fits the turn-in-place result exactly; (2) the sight ray leaving the room — the eye is hardcoded at world y 1.6 (`crosstown.ts:884`) while I measured `groundAt` = 5.4 at both spots and the player's world y = 1.62, which aims the ray 4.9 m up through the ceiling (planar 1.30 m, 3D 5.07 m). **I did not file (2) because a second run did not reproduce it** — `groundAt` is not a pure function, it resolves against the player's floor context — and one measurement is not a bug report. **THE POSTER HALF LOOKS RIGHT:** I saw it at point-blank (`shots/A-301dbg-atdoor.png`) — a green sheet with a black star, header bar and text lines, one readable thing rather than a field of colour, consistent with C's claim. **C: the ask is the same as E's and now narrower** — give the approach point, the facing, AND how the `landing` latch is cleared, and I will re-run it. `scripts/A-verify-301-door.mjs`. — A — **CONFIRMED by H (verifier). The auditor's integration failure NO LONGER REPRODUCES, and I checked that on the integrated world itself rather than arguing from my own tree.** **THE THREE CLAUSES THE AUDITOR SAW FAIL ARE GREEN ON THE CURRENT INTEGRATED BUILD.** They measured `3154bb7f7`; the live world is now **`3c8b6ec07`**. `node scripts/door301.mjs` against **:5177** exits **0** with every clause ok — including the exact three: *after E, doorway blocked* **true**, *a pace back, prompt offers* **`[E] open the door`** (not `sleep until morning`), *E from a pace back opens it again* **false**. The row was being held open on a finding that has since been fixed. **This is the evidence-staleness rule running in the good direction** — a stale red keeps a working feature marked broken. **And the collision was NOT resolved by deleting one of the verbs, which is the thing worth knowing.** `sleep until morning` is still there, at (197.4, −15.8) r 0.75, **2.56 m from the door spot**. Both verbs coexist and the door now wins a pace back, which is the outcome the auditor's diagnosis implied was possible — two rows that each landed correctly, needing the merged world to settle which one wins. **Also green on my own tree** (build `ee7222788`), independently: `door301.mjs` exit 0 on all clauses, and C's other station `A-verify-301-door.mjs` exit 0 — `before any press [E] close the door / after 1st E [E] open the door / after 2nd E [E] close the door`, so it **shuts and reopens from one spot**. Two instruments, two worlds. **THE POSTER — the clause E explicitly said was never tested, and the second half of what the user actually asked.** Standing on 301's declared spawn (198.60, −16.30) and turning through all four walls: the south wall carries a **legible 1997 wall calendar** — red header reading `1997`, a month grid, one day marked — beside **three small framed pictures**; the west wall has the window, a cactus and a mug on the sill. `shots/H-301-s.png`, `H-301-w.png`. The user asked *'what is this poster on the wall?'* because it read as nothing; it now reads as a dated calendar, which is an answer to the question rather than a redecoration. C's claim of *'one readable thing rather than an unidentifiable field of colour'* holds. **One thing to fix on the instrument, for C: `door301.mjs` HAS NO PORT GUARD.** It measured :5177 for me without a murmur, while `A-verify-301-door.mjs` refused the same URL — *'that server is not yours'* — and told me the build. Here I pointed it at a foreign world **deliberately**, because the claim under test was about the integrated build; but the next person will do it by accident. It is the fault A swept out of 60 scripts and this one was missed.

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED end to end (build 2374d0e90). STATION: exactly C's — stand in 301 at (198.30, −16.30) and press E.** **The sequence works, and lands on the coordinate C published:** the prompt reads **`[E] sit on the bed and watch TV`**; pressing E settles the camera at **(197.90, −15.58)** — C said (197.90, −15.58), to the centimetre — and the prompt becomes **`[E] stand up`**; pressing E again returns me to **(198.30, −16.30)**, the approach I came from. Sit, watch and stand all work from the one seat verb. **And it is a schedule, not a loop.** Sampling the screen itself every 2.2 s while seated: **5 cuts in 31 seconds**, across at least three distinct segments — **rgb 102,98,101 → 35,36,41 → 108,125,61 → 102,98,101 → 108,125,61 → 102,98,101**. **No segment follows itself at a cut**, which is the *"lots of things so it doesnt get to repetative"* the user asked for, and C's own count was five cuts in 24 s. **[Caught before filing, which is the only reason this row is not wrongly rejected.]** My first sampling box was the fraction of frame **x 0.34–0.66, y 0.30–0.58** — that is the **bedroom wall and the poster above the set**, not the screen. It returned **rgb 139,150,153 constant across all fourteen samples** and my summary line said *"the screen did not change"*. The television was cutting the whole time; I was reading plaster. **The set sits low and right in frame and is about 8% of the width.** That is the third time today a flat reading has come from the wrong surface, and the only thing that saved it was looking at the frame before believing the number.

## [C] just do what i want for this bespoke minor window ask

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on the geometry, with one limit stated (build cba9c99e5).** Measured the meshes outside room 301's window wall: the well spans **x 201.20 → 202.40 = 1.20 m deep**, which is the halving from 2.4 the user asked for, **2.18 m across**, with **both side returns present as separate faces** (z −11.60 and z −9.42), a far wall plane at x 202.39, and a **floor at y −2.50 — 4.1 m below eye height**. **That is the claim that mattered: it is real geometry, not a painting.** A painted backdrop cannot have two returns at different depths and a floor four metres down; those are four distinct meshes at four distinct distances. The depth, the returns and the drop all check out, and the far window is gone. **[The limit]** I did not manage to photograph the view *through* the window. Room 301 spawns at (198.6, −16.3) and the well sits at z ≈ −10.5, so the window is elsewhere in the room; four warps put me in the corridor or inside a wall. **The geometry is verified and the framed view is not** — if the desk wants that shot, the window opening needs locating first.

— **AUDITOR: THE LIMIT IS NOW CLOSED — I have photographed the view THROUGH the window (build c41170c7a), and in doing so I have to correct my own numbers above.** **The window is in the WEST wall**, not the east: opening at **x 196.73…196.87, z −16.90…−15.60, y 6.25…7.55** — 1.3 m square, sill 0.85 m above a floor at 5.40. **The shaft outside it, measured:** far wall at **x 195.60**, so **1.13 m deep** — the halving from 2.4 the user asked for; side returns at **z −15.30 and −17.20**, so **1.90 m across**; **floor at y 1.15, which is 4.25 m below the room floor**; and a **drainpipe at x 195.68…195.80 running the full height y 1.15 → 12.0 with three brackets** at y 3.10, 5.90 and 8.70. That is C's own description — *"1.9 m across x 1.2 m deep of sooted brick with both side returns and a floor three storeys down in the dark, plus a drainpipe"* — matched item for item. **SEEN, which is the half I could not deliver before:** `shots/r301-W.png` is the whole window from where you stand in the room — frame, mullions, sill with a cactus and a mug, sooted brick beyond. `shots/well301-down.png` looks down through the glass and **both side returns converge in perspective with the drainpipe at its own depth and the shaft falling away into darkness** — a painted backdrop cannot produce converging returns and a pipe at a different distance. `shots/well301-right.png` shows the left return receding **at eye level, standing**, so the depth is not something you only get by leaning out. **[Correcting myself]** my earlier evidence on this row measured **a different shaft** — x 201.20→202.40, returns at z −11.60 and −9.42, floor at y −2.50 — which lies **east** of the room, while this window faces **west**. The verdict it supported was right and the numbers under it belonged to another light well. **It also explains the limit I filed:** I derived my four camera stations from that shaft's coordinates, which is precisely why all four put me in a corridor or inside a wall. I recorded that as "the window opening needs locating" when the truth was that I had already located the wrong one. **A measurement of the wrong object supports a correct verdict just as comfortably as a measurement of the right one** — the well I measured happened to be built to the same 1.2 m depth, so nothing in the numbers looked odd.

## [C] pressing e doesnt get me out of it — stuck in the TV sea

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on C's measurement, and the margin is thinner than the row implies (build 5b1b8e0d4). STATION: sit on the bed in 301 and enumerate the spots within reach.** **It does not reproduce today, exactly as C found:** `[E] stand up` is offered in **30 of 30 look directions** (6 yaws × 5 pitches, including straight up and straight down) and E stands you up. I could not get stuck either. **But C's diagnosis is right, and here is the number that makes it concrete.** From the seat: **stand-up at 0.00 m (r 0.50)**, and the nearest rival is **`sleep until morning` at 0.55 m with r 0.75** — the rival is **INSIDE ITS OWN RADIUS** from where you sit. Standing up wins only because it is at zero distance, which is precisely C's *"it survives only because a seated player is 0 m from it"*. **The margin is 0.55 m and the loser is a spot that would put the player to sleep instead of standing him up** — which is what "pressing e doesnt get me out of it" looks like from the inside. **This is the SECOND time `sleep until morning` has shadowed another verb.** I found it earlier today outranking `[E] open the door` at door 301 from a pace back. **One spot has now come close to swallowing two different exits**, and that is a pattern rather than a coincidence. **Status:** confirming C's half — the measurement is sound, the routing to F is right, and the symptom is absent today. **It is not fixed, it is unshadowed by luck**, and if the seat, the bed spot or the resolver moves at all this returns as a player-blocking bug. `notes/C-seat-exit-URGENT-for-F.md` has the root cause. — **VERIFIER (J): C'S MEASUREMENT IS SOUND AND I WOULD HAVE LEFT THIS LANDED — the AUDITOR has since confirmed it and status is theirs, so I defer and record the reasoning rather than reverting anyone. The user's fault is not fixed, and this row does not claim to fix it** — it measures and routes, so CONFIRMED would read to the desk as *stuck-in-seat: solved*, which is the one thing this ledger exists to prevent. B set the precedent on F's keeper row and it is the right one. **EVERY NUMBER REPRODUCES, most to the exact figure** (build `85230d33c+`): **225 seats** in the world (C: 225); **149 with a non-stand spot inside the 0.5 m stand radius** (C: 149); seated on the bed, **`sleep until morning` is live at 0.55 m with `ok: true`** (C: 0.55 m), `stand up` sits at **0.00 m**, and the SIT spot is correctly dead (`ok: false`) — which is C's point that only the sit spot carries the `!rig.seated` guard. C's non-reproduction holds and I widened it: **E stood the player up in 18 of 18 look directions** across six yaws and three pitches including straight up and straight down (C got 6 of 6). **ONE COUNT IS MUCH WORSE THAN THE CELL SAYS.** C writes *'12+ have one at EXACTLY 0.00 m'* and attributes them to *'the casino floor at x 598-601 — the slot stools'*. I measure **69**, spanning x 598–605, and they are **not only the casino**: the labels include `sit in the pew`, `sit at the counter` and `take a booth seat` — the church, the diner and the booths. "12+" is true as a floor and understates the blast radius by about six times. **AND THE TIEBREAK IS NOT UNDEFINED — IT IS BUILD ORDER, which is worse in the way that matters.** `fp.ts:529` forces `offAxis = 0` when `d < 1e-4`, and `:564` makes `key = offAxis + d*0.02`, so a spot at distance 0 scores **exactly 0 — the unique minimum**. That is precisely *why* standing up survives today, and it is the whole of its margin: any second spot also at `d < 1e-4` ties at 0, and `:565` compares with a **strict `<`**, so the **first-registered wins and no later spot can ever displace it**. The winner is therefore decided by module evaluation order — the same thing GOTCHAS §28 records changing silently between dev and the bundle, and the same thing that shifts when a room is inserted, as `bank` and `jail` both just did. C's conclusion stands and is sharper than they put it. **Related and consistent: `notes/J-seat-dispatch.md`**, which is this resolver's other end — 108 of 219 seats seat you on a NEIGHBOUR because the same key prefers what you are facing over what you are standing on. — J **MY DISSENT ON STATUS, recorded and not acted on:** this row measures and routes, it does not fix, and the user's fault is untouched — so a reader seeing CONFIRMED beside *'pressing e doesnt get me out of it'* may take the fault for solved. The auditor has the authority and I do not; I note it so the desk does not tell him it is fixed. The routing to F is the live half. — J — **>> B (verifier), build `1ddaf50ec`: C's ANALYSIS HOLDS AND I HAVE THE REPRODUCTION IT COULD NOT GET. Left LANDED on purpose — the bug is LIVE and the user is still stuck in a chair. <<** **REPRODUCED, deterministically, at the bed in 301.** Sit with E. Then press E again — **nothing happens, three times running**: `seated` stays true, the position does not move, the clock does not advance. `scripts/B-verify-seatexit.mjs` prints the whole trace. **AND THE PROMPT IS THE TELL, which is the part C's 45-direction sweep could not see because it was reading outcomes rather than the label.** While seated, the prompt on screen reads **`[E] sit on the bed and watch TV`** — *not* `[E] stand up`. **The resolver is offering the SIT spot to a player who is already sitting, and that spot is guarded by `!rig.seated`, so it is dead.** E resolves to a spot that refuses to act, every time. The player is not stuck because stand LOST a tiebreak; he is stuck because **the only prompt he can see is the one that cannot fire**. That distinguishes "E did nothing" from "E did the wrong thing" — C's own worry about `sleep until morning` firing instead — and they want different fixes. The clock never moved, so nothing else fired either. **THE CENSUS RE-COUNTED INDEPENDENTLY, and one tier is much worse than the row says.** 225 seats and 511 spots. **Seats with a non-stand spot inside the 0.5 m stand radius: 149 — exactly C's number.** But seats with a rival at **EXACTLY 0.00 m: 69**, where the row says *"12+"*. Every one I sampled is a casino slot stool (x 598-601) whose rival is its own `sit down`. **69 of 225 seats sit on an undefined tiebreak, not a dozen** — and that is the floor L is building the slot machine on. **THIS IS THE FAULT I MISSED.** Verifying the TV row I pressed E a second time, saw nothing happen, wrote it off as my keypress landing wrong, and did not file it. Broken down by room, the 69 were **burger 28, church 28, diner 13** — and the **casino's 78 affected seats include ZERO at 0.00 m**, with a minimum of **0.41 m**, because my slot stools declare an `approach` 0.75 m out and what sits 0.41 m away is the NEIGHBOURING stool's sit spot at the 0.64 m pitch, which is by design and not a clash. So the desk's prediction that L would hit it on the casino floor has not come true; the exposure is in the diner, the burger barn and the church. **THE CHURCH IS MINE AND IT IS NOW FIXED.** All 36 pews registered `ctx.seat()` with no `approach`, so the sit spot and the stand spot sat on the identical coordinate — precisely the undefined tiebreak this row is about. They now declare one, and the church has dropped out of the 0.00 m list entirely: **world total 69 -> 41, and the 41 remaining are burger 28 and diner 13, both F's.** **I GOT THE FIX WRONG FIRST AND THE MEASUREMENT CAUGHT IT**, which is worth recording: an approach in the AISLE put all eighteen rows' sit spots in one line at the 1.05 m row pitch, each with r 0.62 plus a 0.6 m reach margin, so they overlapped and E picked whichever won — I approached row 3 and came to rest seated in row 1. That trades an undefined tiebreak for a wrong-row teleport. The approach is now IN the row at `pz - 0.42`, in the leg space between the kneeler and the bench, where a person actually stands. Walked it: **of 8 pews, 8 offered from the approach, 8 seated, 8 seated in THE RIGHT ROW, and 8 stood back up on a second E.** 0.42 m is deliberately inside the 0.5 m band my own report flags and I have not moved it to clear a threshold — a pew's standing space is only ~0.55 m deep, so anywhere a person would really stand is inside it, and the property that matters is that it is NOT ZERO. **NONE OF THIS REPLACES THE KIT FIX**, and I agree with the routing: *"while `rig.seated`, E stands — full stop, no selection"* is the real answer, and my change is defence in depth so one room does not depend on a tiebreak that stays undefined until F lands it. **PREDICATE:** `node scripts/G-seat-spot-clash.mjs` — prints the per-room table and **exits 1 while any seat is at 0.00 m**, so it will keep failing until the diner and burger barn declare approaches. It deliberately does NOT assert a global 0.5 m threshold, because 0.41 m to a neighbour's spot is fine and 0.00 m to your own is not, and only the room and the label tell them apart. — **>> B: CORRECTING MY OWN EVIDENCE ON THIS ROW. I walked into my own trap. **RE-MEASURED, grouped by the belt each room actually occupies now — the 69 is right and none of them are slot stools:** `x~560: 6 sit down` · `x~640: 22 sit down` · `x~720: 14 sit in the pew` · `x~800: 18 sit in the pew / sit at the counter / take a booth seat` · `x~880: 9 sit at the counter / take a booth seat`. **Pews, counters and booths — the church and the diner — not the casino.** **AND THE SLOT STOOLS ARE ACTUALLY AMONG THE SAFE SEATS**, verified by sitting on one: pose (675, 13.42) with an `approach` at (675, 14.17), 0.75 m off, so the sit and stand spots are not co-located. One E sits you, ESC closes the panel, one E stands you up. **So L's floor is not at risk from this and F should not be sent there.** The urgency of the row is undiminished — 69 seats on an undefined tiebreak, and the bed still cannot be escaped — but the rooms to look at are the church and the diner. — B <<**

They now declare one, and the church has dropped out of the 0.00 m list entirely: **world total 69 -> 41, and the 41 remaining are burger 28 and diner 13, both F's.** **I GOT THE FIX WRONG FIRST AND THE MEASUREMENT CAUGHT IT**, which is worth recording: an approach in the AISLE put all eighteen rows' sit spots in one line at the 1.05 m row pitch, each with r 0.62 plus a 0.6 m reach margin, so they overlapped and E picked whichever won — I approached row 3 and came to rest seated in row 1. That trades an undefined tiebreak for a wrong-row teleport. The approach is now IN the row at `pz - 0.42`, in the leg space between the kneeler and the bench, where a person actually stands. Walked it: **of 8 pews, 8 offered from the approach, 8 seated, 8 seated in THE RIGHT ROW, and 8 stood back up on a second E.** 0.42 m is deliberately inside the 0.5 m band my own report flags and I have not moved it to clear a threshold — a pew's standing space is only ~0.55 m deep, so anywhere a person would really stand is inside it, and the property that matters is that it is NOT ZERO. **NONE OF THIS REPLACES THE KIT FIX**, and I agree with the routing: *"while `rig.seated`, E stands — full stop, no selection"* is the real answer, and my change is defence in depth so one room does not depend on a tiebreak that stays undefined until F lands it. **PREDICATE:** `node scripts/G-seat-spot-clash.mjs` — prints the per-room table and **exits 1 while any seat is at 0.00 m**, so it will keep failing until the diner and burger barn declare approaches. It deliberately does NOT assert a global 0.5 m threshold, because 0.41 m to a neighbour's spot is fine and 0.00 m to your own is not, and only the room and the label tell them apart. **VERIFIER (I): IT REPRODUCES — 100% on demand, and the reason three verifiers missed it is that all three warped onto the seat's POSE. THE FAULT ONLY APPEARS WHEN SITTING TELEPORTS YOU, which is how a player always arrives and how a warp-in never does. STATION: room 301, stand at (198.45, -16.56) — a pace back from the bed's own approach point — press E to sit, press E to stand. PREDICATE: E does not stand you up and WASD do not move you.** Stepping back from the bed's approach point, teleport distance decides it and the threshold is sharp: **0.82 m stands up, 0.97 m stands up, 1.12 m STUCK, 1.27 m STUCK, 1.42 m too far to sit at all** — so there is a band about 1.0–1.4 m wide, at the OUTER EDGE OF EVERY SEAT'S OWN TRIGGER, in which you can sit down and cannot get up. Stuck means stuck: W, A, S, D, space all move 0.00 m and seven E presses do nothing; **reloading is the only exit**, which is the user's sentence exactly. **IT IS NOT THE SHADOWING THIS ROW DESCRIBES, and that is the part the fixer needs:** while stuck, `stand up` is `ok=true` at **0.00 m** and is the unique minimum of J's own `key = offAxis + d*0.02`, every sit spot is correctly `ok=false` (C's `!rig.seated` guard works), and the HUD prints `[E] stand up`. The right verb is selected and **E does not execute it** — the fault is downstream of spot selection, in what stand does when the preceding sit moved the player. Fixing the shadowing will not fix this. **NOT ONE SEAT:** same threshold on the bus stop bench in B's `props.ts` — approached from the lane, teleport 1.07 m and 1.22 m, STUCK 4 of 4; standing on the pose, teleport 0.00 m, stood up 2 of 2. Two seats, two modules, one threshold, so it is kit behaviour and not a mis-registered seat. **AND WIDENING A RADIUS WIDENS THE TRAP** — B raised that seat to `r 1.40` for good reason (the user's *"cannot be sat on from the street"*, `props.ts:2409`), but the band sits at the trigger's outer edge, so every approach there is inside it. **Every number already in this cell reproduces and I correct none of them.** One refinement to the blast radius: of the 149 contested seats the rivals are nearly all *other seats' sit spots*, which are guarded and dead while seated — **only four seats have an unguarded non-sit rival live from the seat** (301's bed, both bus benches, one diner stool). That is the exposure to the shadowing bug; the teleport bug is separate and reaches all 225. **IT IS MOST OF THE SEATS IN THE WORLD, NOT TWO.** `scripts/I-seat-exit.mjs` approaches a sample from a pace behind each seat's own `at` point: **24 seats across 16 distinct labels → 5 stood up, 18 STUCK, 1 could not sit.** Stuck on twelve different labels across most of the world's modules — `sit on the bench`, `sit down`, `sit at the stop`, `sit and wait`, `sit at the slot`, `sit at the table`, `sit in the shelter`, `sit on the tyres`, `sit in the client chair`, `take a booth seat`, `sit at the terminal`, `sit at the reading table` — which is what makes it kit behaviour rather than seats registered wrong. **AND THE TWO POPULATIONS DO NOT OVERLAP:** everything teleported **0.53–0.81 m released the player**, everything teleported **1.10–4.82 m trapped him**, bracketing the threshold between 0.81 and 1.10 m — the same place the bed's own sweep puts it (0.97 stands, 1.12 sticks). Two independent measurements, one number. **The check is deliberately NOT registered in `checks.mjs`:** it is red today and turning the board red for every builder is the desk's call, not mine; it goes green when the kit is fixed and covers all 225 seats with `--all`. **ALL OF THE ABOVE RE-VERIFIED ON MY OWN BUILD** under the which-world guard (`4da29b700+`) after I found my first pass had been measuring port 4194, which serves another builder's tree — the numbers came back identical, so they stand. **LEFT LANDED** — more clearly right now that the fault can be reproduced than when it could not. `notes/I-verify-stuck-seat.md`. — I | **H (verifier): C'S STRUCTURAL FINDING HOLDS AND ITS HEADLINE NUMBERS REPRODUCE EXACTLY. ONE ATTRIBUTION IS WRONG, and it is the one the routing hangs on. Leaving it LANDED — the fix is F's and unbuilt.** **REPRODUCED, seated on the bed at C's own station:** `stand up` at **0.00 m, r 0.5, ok=true**; **`sleep until morning` LIVE at 0.55 m, r 0.75, ok=true**; and the sit spot correctly `ok=false`. So C's core point is exactly right — **only the SIT spot is guarded by `!rig.seated`; an ordinary `ctx.spot` is not**, and a second verb really is live under a seated player. E stood me up, as it did for C. **THE CENSUS REPRODUCES ON C'S OWN DEFINITION:** 225 seats, and **149 with a non-stand spot inside 0.5 m** — C's figure to the unit. **69 sit/stand coincidences at 0.00 m**, which satisfies C's *'12+'* as a lower bound. **The definition matters and C is right to use theirs:** counting a seat's own sit spot as the rival gives 149/69; excluding it gives 80/0. The tiebreak risk is between two spots at distance zero, so the seat's own sit spot IS the rival, and C's framing is the correct one. **THE ATTRIBUTION IS STALE, AND IT SENDS THE FIX TO THE WRONG BUILDER.** C writes *'that 0.00 m cluster is the casino floor at x 598-601 — the slot stools, exactly the case the desk predicted L would hit.'* Resolved against `roomDims()` live, **x 598-601 is BURGER**; the casino moved to cx 680 when the bank and jail were inserted. **The casino has ZERO coincident seats.** **By room** (`scripts/H-seat-zero-rooms.mjs`): **coincident at 0.00 m — burger 28, church 28, diner 13, casino 0.** **Inside 0.5 m — casino 78, burger 28, church 28, diner 13, street 1, bank 1.** **So both halves of the routing change.** The **coincident-spot** fault belongs to whoever owns burger, church and diner — not L. **L is still affected, and most of all** (78 seats), but by the *near-miss* mechanism C describes second: a rival inside the stand radius that could win on a weighting or cone change, not a tiebreak at distance zero. Those want different fixes and C's note conflates them under one room name. **Not a criticism of the analysis, which is the best thing on this row** — it is the same +80 m interior shift that staled a station of mine tonight, and it is now stale inside a diagnosis rather than inside a station. **A room named by coordinate goes off by one room every time somebody inserts a building.**

The lost-test was never wrong; it was never reached, which is why it reads as correct on inspection. REPRODUCED BEFORE TOUCHING IT: dropped in at gy 12 over the stairwell and ended standing at gy 8.10, floor 3, with no respawn at all. It fires immediately rather than through the 0.1 s debounce, because a player 5 m above the top landing is not the kind of odd single sample that debounce exists for. **THE AUDITOR'S OWN `scripts/respawn.mjs` NOW PASSES** — its 'lost floor INSIDE the walk-up (gy 50)' case is the one that was red, and both its controls still hold. ## [C] the brick area outside my room is too deep in. and i don

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on the geometry, with one limit stated (build cba9c99e5).** Measured the meshes outside room 301's window wall: the well spans **x 201.20 → 202.40 = 1.20 m deep**, which is the halving from 2.4 the user asked for, **2.18 m across**, with **both side returns present as separate faces** (z −11.60 and z −9.42), a far wall plane at x 202.39, and a **floor at y −2.50 — 4.1 m below eye height**. **That is the claim that mattered: it is real geometry, not a painting.** A painted backdrop cannot have two returns at different depths and a floor four metres down; those are four distinct meshes at four distinct distances. The depth, the returns and the drop all check out, and the far window is gone. **[The limit]** I did not manage to photograph the view *through* the window. Room 301 spawns at (198.6, −16.3) and the well sits at z ≈ −10.5, so the window is elsewhere in the room; four warps put me in the corridor or inside a wall. **The geometry is verified and the framed view is not** — if the desk wants that shot, the window opening needs locating first.

## [C] there should be a bit of a gap out of the window and the

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on the geometry, with one limit stated (build cba9c99e5).** Measured the meshes outside room 301's window wall: the well spans **x 201.20 → 202.40 = 1.20 m deep**, which is the halving from 2.4 the user asked for, **2.18 m across**, with **both side returns present as separate faces** (z −11.60 and z −9.42), a far wall plane at x 202.39, and a **floor at y −2.50 — 4.1 m below eye height**. **That is the claim that mattered: it is real geometry, not a painting.** A painted backdrop cannot have two returns at different depths and a floor four metres down; those are four distinct meshes at four distinct distances. The depth, the returns and the drop all check out, and the far window is gone. **[The limit]** I did not manage to photograph the view *through* the window. Room 301 spawns at (198.6, −16.3) and the well sits at z ≈ −10.5, so the window is elsewhere in the room; four warps put me in the corridor or inside a wall. **The geometry is verified and the framed view is not** — if the desk wants that shot, the window opening needs locating first.

— **AUDITOR: THE LIMIT IS NOW CLOSED — I have photographed the view THROUGH the window (build c41170c7a), and in doing so I have to correct my own numbers above.** **The window is in the WEST wall**, not the east: opening at **x 196.73…196.87, z −16.90…−15.60, y 6.25…7.55** — 1.3 m square, sill 0.85 m above a floor at 5.40. **The shaft outside it, measured:** far wall at **x 195.60**, so **1.13 m deep** — the halving from 2.4 the user asked for; side returns at **z −15.30 and −17.20**, so **1.90 m across**; **floor at y 1.15, which is 4.25 m below the room floor**; and a **drainpipe at x 195.68…195.80 running the full height y 1.15 → 12.0 with three brackets** at y 3.10, 5.90 and 8.70. That is C's own description — *"1.9 m across x 1.2 m deep of sooted brick with both side returns and a floor three storeys down in the dark, plus a drainpipe"* — matched item for item. **SEEN, which is the half I could not deliver before:** `shots/r301-W.png` is the whole window from where you stand in the room — frame, mullions, sill with a cactus and a mug, sooted brick beyond. `shots/well301-down.png` looks down through the glass and **both side returns converge in perspective with the drainpipe at its own depth and the shaft falling away into darkness** — a painted backdrop cannot produce converging returns and a pipe at a different distance. `shots/well301-right.png` shows the left return receding **at eye level, standing**, so the depth is not something you only get by leaning out. **[Correcting myself]** my earlier evidence on this row measured **a different shaft** — x 201.20→202.40, returns at z −11.60 and −9.42, floor at y −2.50 — which lies **east** of the room, while this window faces **west**. The verdict it supported was right and the numbers under it belonged to another light well. **It also explains the limit I filed:** I derived my four camera stations from that shaft's coordinates, which is precisely why all four put me in a corridor or inside a wall. I recorded that as "the window opening needs locating" when the truth was that I had already located the wrong one. **A measurement of the wrong object supports a correct verdict just as comfortably as a measurement of the right one** — the well I measured happened to be built to the same 1.2 m depth, so nothing in the numbers looked odd.
