## [G] i asked for more expansive interiors for casino and for 

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED against where the rooms were when the user complained (build 5ec85ac84).** Casino **95 → 209 m²** and hotel **99 → 286 m²** — 2.2× and 2.9×. The hotel is now the most open room in the world: median clear aisle **11.0 m**, minimum **5.27 m**, **96% free floor**, and `shots/ti-hotel.png` shows a lobby that reads as grand — patterned carpet the full 26 m, receding pendants, reception desk, gold picture rail. Expansive, delivered. **Two things for G, neither a rejection.** (1) **The added depth is unfurnished:** 96% free floor is the highest of the ten, and the near half of the lobby is bare carpet with everything at the far end. The room is expansive in area and sparse in content, which is a different thing from expansive. (2) **The footprint overrun moved rather than went away.** I filed the casino at **1.96×** its building footprint earlier; it is now **1.27×** (11 × 19 against 11.55 × 14.30) — better. But the hotel went **0.58× → 1.67×** (11 × 26 against 12.00 × 14.30). G's note says *"depth was the free axis … nothing was ever behind these rooms"*, and that is **true of the interior belt and false of the street**: the belt has 80 m of clear space behind every room, but the building on the side street is 14.3 m deep. A 26 m lobby does not fit inside it. Church 0.94× is the one that still fits. scripts/roomaisle.mjs, bigtwo.mjs, twoint.mjs.

## [G] instead of calling the casino golden aces call it SEVENS

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 8c1b58dbb), and the hazard G named caught ME.** **The rename is functional:** the live door registry now reads **BODEGA, BURGER BARN, SEVENS, ST BRIGID, DINER, HOTEL ORPHEUS, LIBRARY, PAWN, A-1 TAX, THRIFT** — the casino answers to SEVENS at runtime, which is the only test that matters. **Six occurrences of the old name survive in `src/` and `scripts/`, and G declared three of them.** `doors.ts:129`, `doors-declared.mjs:13` and `no-import-cycles.mjs:12` are the deliberate ones, kept so the incident they record still reads correctly — the room was lost through a mismatch on the OLD name, and a blind rename would have made them claim SEVENS was lost. **Two more are in `vice.ts:836` and `vice.ts:1236`**, both comments describing the old two-line sign layout; G did not list them and they are harmless, but the count is five comments, not three. **The sixth was FUNCTIONAL and it was mine.** `scripts/doorside.mjs` matched the casino with `/ACES/i` — written by me earlier today, before the rename — so after it the pattern matched nothing and the casino **dropped silently out of my door-handedness table as "no exterior frontage published"**. That is word for word the failure G predicted when it refused a naive string swap: *"it would have tested a dead string, found nothing and printed '0 of them' as a measurement."* **G was right that the bare `ACES` was the real hazard, and my own script is the proof.** Fixed, and it now **exits 3 if any building pattern matches nothing**, so the next rename cannot quietly shrink the population instead of failing. — **VERIFIER (J) CONFIRMED (build `f03c96ba0+`, and MEASURED IN THE BUILT BUNDLE — `vite preview` over `dist`, not the dev server).** A rename is only interesting where it can break something, so I checked the three places it can. **(1) THE DOOR SURVIVES THE BUNDLE**, which is this room's own recorded casualty (GOTCHAS §28 — GOLDEN ACES was the door that vanished from the built output through a name mismatch). Independently: `__ct.doors()` in the bundle returns **10 declared doors, one of them SEVENS at (51.29, −96), and ZERO carrying the old name**; walked up to its stand point, prompt read **`[E] into SEVENS`**, and E put me at x 600 — inside the interior belt. `__ct.rooms()` lists all ten. **(2) NO FUNCTIONAL OLD NAME.** grep over `src/` and `scripts/`: every `ACES` hit is `THREE.ACESFilmicToneMapping` or the word FACES. **(3) THE BLADE IS NOT MIRRORED**, which is where a re-drawn blade on THIS project goes wrong — §10 and §35, and it has shipped twice. Read structurally off the world: two planes at x 55.87 and 56.23, `rotation.y` ∓π/2, **`side: FrontSide` (not DoubleSide) and BOTH `map.repeat.x = +1`** — §35's own predicate is that back-to-back planes reading correctly have the SAME sign. Then read with my eyes from the **west at (44, −96.67), pitch 0.72**: it reads **SEVENS**, top to bottom, unmirrored, in lit bulbs. And `G-vice-walk` re-run by me against the bundle: **18/18**, including *"the two faces of each blade carry the SAME texture, not a mirrored one"*. **STATION, which this cell did not give and now does:** stand at **(44, −96.67) facing +x, pitch 0.72, clock 21:30** for the blade, and at **(51.29, −96.75)** for the door. **TWO THINGS I DID NOT SETTLE, said rather than glossed:** (a) I could NOT read the blade's EAST face in the world — four vantages (60,−99.5), (62,−99), (64,−98), (60,−101) and x 68 are all blocked by the neighbouring mass, so that face is covered by the structural test and by G-vice-walk and not by my eyes. Whether a blade face with no public vantage is worth having is a PLACEMENT question, pre-existing and not what this row changed — recorded, not routed. (b) The cell says *"Three old-name occurrences are left on purpose"*; there are **FOUR**. `scripts/floatshots.mjs:9` carries `// 2 golden aces legs` plus two `F-aces-*` shot filenames. It is non-functional — a comment and two output paths, exactly the category G already noted and left for `G-vice-shots.mjs` — so the row's substance stands, but the count in the cell is wrong and the fourth does not carry the *"SEVENS (then called GOLDEN ACES)"* wording the other three were given. — J **SECOND, INDEPENDENT PASS.** Another verifier reached this row in parallel and I did not see their verdict until the rebase. Both are kept rather than one overwriting the other — they checked different things (theirs the population guard, mine the BUNDLE door, the grep and the blade's handedness), so the two together cover more of the rename than either alone. — J

## [G] make the library interior larger and more ambitious / wh

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED, and my own mid-flight warning on this row is now RESOLVED (build 7a2c9befc).** I warned while this was in flight that the library had doubled in area but its **median clear aisle had fallen to 2.10 m, the narrowest of all ten rooms** — *"the space was not consumed; it was cut into strips."* Re-measured with one definition applied to all ten rooms: **library 440 m², 88% free floor, median passage 5.25 m** — **sixth of ten**, with only church 2.75, bodega 3.00, diner 3.00, thrift 3.50 and burger 3.75 below it. The strips are gone and the room got bigger again. **The metric matters more than the number, so here it is explicitly.** *Passage width*: for every free cell, the clear run through it along x and along z, taking the **smaller** — how wide the way is where you stand. I first used *widest clear gap on a line* and it ranked the library **most open of all ten at 12.36 m**, because that measure calls a room open if **one strip of it is** — the exact failure I had warned about, and my instrument could not see it. **A metric that cannot express the fault you are looking for will always clear it.**

## [G] pews in the church clip into the confession booths, lets

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED, measured and looked at (build fcc75823a).** **No pew touches a booth.** Classified by SHAPE rather than by name — booth: taller than 1.6 m with a 1–3.5 m footprint; pew: long, low, seat height — giving **1 booth (2.42 m tall) and 86 pew meshes**. Closest booth-to-pew gap **1.025 m**, between the booth at (685.8, 7.5) and the pew at (683.4, 5.1); **pairs actually overlapping: 0**. **Seen from inside, both sides of the nave:** `shots/ch-west.png` shows the booth standing clear against the west wall with an unbroken strip of stone floor between it and the last pew; `shots/ch-east.png` shows the east aisle with no booth intersection either. Stained glass, stations of the cross and the votive stand all read normally, so the room is being drawn as intended. **[My first instrument could not fail, and I nearly published its answer.]** I reused the assembly-overlap test that worked in the park, which groups meshes by walking up the scene graph. **Every mesh in the church shares one parent**, so it collapsed the entire interior into ONE assembly and reported **0 interpenetrating pairs** — a clean pass that had compared nothing with nothing. That is the exact shape I have been finding in other people's checks all session, written into my own within the hour. It now returns **−1, "cannot answer"**, when it has fewer than three assemblies to compare, and the verdict above comes from shape classification instead. **A grouping that returns one group is not a measurement of zero faults.**

## [H] "im literally stuck here" — parked cars leaving a gap th

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 82eb63980) — and the reconciliation is worth more than the count.** My gap scan finds **more** band gaps than H's: 412 colliders, 431 facing pairs within 1.6 m, **148 gaps in the 0.40–0.95 m band, of which 55 are on the playable block** (93 are the off-map idle vehicle pool H names). So "none on the block" is not what I measure. **It does not matter, and here is why.** I re-ran the whole-world flood as a **disc of radius 0.36** — the player's own footprint — and the walkable world is **8593 m² in ONE connected region**. Three pockets exist and all three were already established as unreachable behind continuous railings (two churchyard side strips and a park bed); **none involves a car**. **Not one of the 55 on-block gaps isolates anything.** **The methodological point:** a gap NARROWER than 0.72 m cannot be entered by the player at all, and a gap WIDER than 0.72 m that can be entered can also be left the same way — unless what lies beyond it is closed. **So a band-gap census cannot decide trapping; only connectivity can.** H's constraint on the parked-car draw is sound and their parked-car figure holds (0 parked-car pairs in the band); the wider count is simply measuring a quantity that does not determine the fault. Both of us arrive at "nobody is trapped", and the flood is the part that proves it. scripts/trapgaps.mjs, stucksweep.mjs.

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 72e469fd6).** The measurable claim verifies exactly: **zero vehicle textures are mipmapped.** Every texture reached through a vehicle group carries `minFilter = NearestFilter` and `generateMipmaps = false` — 201 car textures and 7 bus textures by my count (H says 69 and 14; I dedupe across every vehicle INSTANCE rather than per kind, so the totals differ and the property does not). **Seen at the angle the fault lived at:** `shots/tg-graze.png`, the tailgate viewed from ~7.5 m at a grazing angle, which is where a mipmap drop crawls into a checkerboard. The face is clean — solid body colour, silver bumper strip, two tail lights, a small latch detail, **no checkerboard and no crawling dither**. **Worth keeping from H's note:** four separate places each set `minFilter` by hand, and *"four copies of one rule is the tell it belonged a level up"* — it now lives in `flatT`, which every textured vehicle material is built through. That is the difference between fixing four symptoms and fixing the cause.

## [H] "these people are stuck" — pedestrians frozen IN THE ROA

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 170a5afa6), and it corrects a blind spot in my own crossing row.** Watched 6 walkers for 241 s at 4 Hz, tracking time spent inside the carriageway and whether they were MOVING while there. **Worst stationary spell in the road: 0.0 s** — nobody was frozen for as long as 3 s. Only two walkers entered the road at all (9.8 s and 20.5 s in total, longest unbroken spell 10.3 s, which is what crossing takes), and **0 samples had a walker off the block entirely**, so the overshoot-to-infinity H describes is gone. **The correction:** my earlier crossing confirmation cited *"219 of 8652 samples with a walker in the roadway"* as a positive control proving crossings were happening. **Roadway presence does not distinguish a walker crossing from a walker stranded** — and stranded-in-the-road is precisely the fault in this row. The conclusion was right, but I had not measured what I was claiming; now I have, and the roadway time is all moving.

The east end is not crossed by anything any more. **And the paint has gone with it**, which is the half that had to follow: `shots/xw-far.png` from the same station that photographed the zebra bars earlier today now shows **plain asphalt and a clean kerb**. **H's ordering was right and worth recording**: edge removed while the stripes were still down is harmless paint nobody walks on; **stripes lifted while the edge remained would have been the original fault with its explanation scraped off** — walkers legal by the graph and jaywalking by the world, with nothing on the ground to explain it. | **REPUBLISHED BY H, 2026-07-26 — MY OWN LATER WORK HAS MOVED THESE NUMBERS, so they are restated rather than left reading true.** Everything above described a closed end with **no pavement**, where the honest answer was two dead-end stubs and a 9-hop / 105.6 m detour round the junction. **O's jail has since put a real footway on the far kerb** — 1.89 m of walk, continuous from z −97 to −109 — and O flagged the graph side to me rather than touching it. That is the ground change this cell said had to come first, so the graph change has followed. **THE EDGE IS BACK, AS PAVEMENT, NOT AS A CROSSING.** Two new nodes on the frontage at `SIDE_X1 + IN` (x 56) — `se-jail` and `ne-jail` — and three ORDINARY edges: out along z −109, north up the frontage, back in along z −97. Derived by the file's own one-metre-in-from-the-kerb rule, not hand-placed. **THE NUMBERS THAT REPLACE THE ONES ABOVE:** `s-east → ne-corner` is now **4 hops, 16.0 m, ZERO road-flagged hops** — it was 9 hops / 105.6 m with one junction crossing while the stubs stood, and 2 hops / 12 m straight up the carriageway before any of this. **The stubs are no longer stubs.** **AND THE GROUND UNDER IT WAS MEASURED, not assumed** (`scripts/H-eastend-onfoot.mjs`): **35 points sampled every 0.5 m along the whole route, 0 of them on carriageway.** That check matters because 'no road-flagged hop' only says nobody labelled it a crossing — the deleted edge was unflagged *and* in the road, which was the entire original fault. The kerb is unambiguous here: the carriageway band z −98…−108 reads groundAt **0** out to x 55 and **0.14** from x 55.5, so x 54 was in the road and x 56 is not. **No regression:** 6588 walker samples over 200 s, 403 in the carriageway, **all inside a marked crossing, 0 outside** — the same measure and the same result as when the edge was absent. **Reachability holds from real origins:** 18 origin→destination pairs across w-diner, n-bodega and e-bench to all six east-end nodes all route, and **every road hop on every one of them is one of the two junction crossings** (`scripts/H-eastend-reach.mjs`). `n-bodega → se-jail` is 59.3 m with **zero** road hops, which was impossible before. **One thing I checked and will NOT claim:** no walker was observed on the new leg in 200 s. That is **not** evidence of anything — the world runs **6 walkers**, ~15% of trips are long-range and 2 of ~14 act-nodes sit at the east end, so the expected number of east trips in that window is about **0.6**. I nearly filed 'nobody walks the east end' off the back of it. Routing is deterministic and answers the question; traffic counts at this density cannot.

## [H] the walkable graph walked up the ROAD at the side street

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED — H removed the blocker and the flag now reads true (build cba9c99e5).** I left this LANDED saying an outside test could not read an edge's `road` flag. `netRoute` now returns the edges it walked, and it **discriminates both ways**, which is the part that makes it evidence rather than an echo: `netRoute("s-east","ne-corner")` → **`road: true, half: 1.30, len: 12, crossings: 1`**; `netRoute("w-win1","w-alley")` → **`road: false, half: 0.55, crossings: 0`**. So the east-end edge is flagged as a crossing and carries a crossing's lateral allowance (1.30 against a walk's 0.55), which is exactly the claim. **A reader that returned `true` for everything would have proved nothing; the walk edge coming back false is what makes the crossing edge mean something.** **Worth recording as process:** I declined to confirm this rather than infer the flag from "nobody is stuck there", and named the one accessor that would settle it. H built it and the row closed in a single check. That is the fourth *"cannot answer without a declaration"* of this audit and the first to be answered.

## [H] this guy is floating

— **AUDITOR RE-CONFIRMED after H's crowd regression, build c41170c7a.** H reported that rewriting `citizenPlane` for the seated pose inverted a sign and put the whole crowd 1.66 m underground, which would have made this CONFIRMED false while nobody was looking at it. Re-ran `footpaint.mjs`: **18 citizens, 17 flush with their own ground, 1 exception.** The exception is the **church keeper at −0.18 m**, which is the residual already recorded on the interior-people row (its floor is at 0.18 while the figure sits at 0) — not a new fault and not this row's. **18 is also H's own independent count**, which is worth more than either of us counting alone. **[My instrument was wrong twice and both would have failed this row.]** (1) The headline counted **any mesh sampling a sub-rect of an atlas**, which in this world is stoop steps, hedges and brick as well as people — I photographed the five 'figures' at (−9.2, −13) and they are a **brownstone stoop** (`shots/sunk-side.png`, `shots/sunk-feet.png`), its stepped side cheeks. So '26 of 45 figures off the ground' was a headline over a population that was mostly masonry, while the by-frame-height table underneath it was correct all along. It now headlines the 64-row frames, which are the citizens, and reports the rest as scenery. (2) **`groundAt(x, z) IS NOT A PURE FUNCTION OF x AND z.** In a single page, with the player **never moving** — (198.6, −16.3) at both probes — `groundAt(201.95, −16.5)` returned **5.4 and then 0**, because the player's own ground component went 5.4 → 0 between them. Read cold, that says a citizen is **floating 5.4 m**; walked to, `shots/highfig-up.png` shows it standing on the **301/302 hallway floor** where it belongs. footpaint now re-reads every suspect with the player warped to it, which is the only context in which the answer means anything. Written up for everyone in `notes/groundat-context.md` — every script in `scripts/` that compares a height to `groundAt` at an interior location is reading a context-dependent number.

— **AUDITOR: the re-confirmation above was measured on a STALE BUILD; re-done on HEAD (8e78016c9), and the verdict holds with a correction to my instrument.** Port 4184 runs `vite preview`, which serves the pre-built `dist/` and does **not** pick up source changes; it had been up since 22:11 on build `c41170c7a`. I caught it because `__ct.debugSpots` exists at `crosstown.ts:682` and was **not on the running object** while `advanceClock` and `highlightParity`, its neighbours in the same object literal, both were — **two adjacent keys of one literal cannot disagree in a single build.** After `npm run build` and a restart, `debugSpots` appeared. **On the real build: 22 citizen frames, of which 17 STANDING — and 0 of 17 more than 3 cm off the ground.** The other five are **SEATED**, and that distinction is the correction: four casino gamblers read 0.165 m "off the floor" and each sits at **exactly 0.00 m** from a registered `"sit at the slot"` seat of pose height **0.64** — a bar stool. **Feet that hang above the floor are what sitting on a stool looks like.** "The painted shoe must touch the ground" is a claim about standing people, and checking a seated one against the floor is the same category error as checking a quad bottom instead of a painted foot — the exact mistake this row exists to record. `footpaint.mjs` now splits seated from standing off the seat registry. **The fifth seated figure is the church keeper** at (676.6, −5.65), on a **0.54 m** pew, painted foot 0 against a floor at 0.18 — so the −0.18 residual noted on the interior-people row is real and is about its PLACEMENT (figure at absolute 0, floor at 0.18), not about sitting. **[The disclosure]** three of my confirmations were made after 22:11 and therefore on `c41170c7a`: this one, the park bench-backs row, and the three room-301 window rows. **I re-ran all three on HEAD** — bench backs returned byte-identical numbers (0 of 9, 23.4/5.9 down to 6.15/5.15) and the window well returned identical geometry; only this row moved. **A stale build is not detectable from the measurements it produces** — every number I took was internally consistent — which is why the build stamp has to be read rather than the results trusted. GOTCHAS 26, third time today across the team.

— **AUDITOR, DECAY RE-CHECK after two interior insertions and a rename (build bd915e0cb): this row still holds, with ONE figure I could not settle, routed to O as a question rather than a defect.** **Re-run: 27 standing citizens, 26 flush.** The population has grown from 17 as rooms were added and the convention has held across all of them. **The one exception is in the JAIL, and the number is suggestive:** the three jail figures have quad bases **−0.13, −0.12 and 0.00**. The first two are the `citizenPlane` convention — the quad hangs below the floor by the empty rows under the painted shoe, so the shoe lands on the floor. **The third starts AT the floor, which puts its painted foot 0.120 m up** — and 0.119 m is exactly the atlas padding this row was filed about. **But it is a prisoner at a bunk in a cell** (`shots/jf-odd.png`), and **if he is SEATED, feet off the floor is correct.** My seated-versus-standing split works off the registered seat list, and a bunk in a cell has no `[E] sit` spot, so he is classified as standing by default. **I could not get a side view to settle it — the cell is too small for my camera offsets and both attempts put me inside a wall.** **So: not a claimed regression.** For O, the question is one line: **is the cell figure built through `citizenPlane` like the other two, or placed at floor level?** If it is seated the answer is "correct, ignore me"; if it is standing it is the 12 cm float this row exists to prevent, in a room the row predates.

## [H] tons of people always get stuck at this cross walk. the 

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED, and I measured the world the USER PLAYS, which answers H's own open question (5177, and 4184 for comparison).** H wrote *"Desk: worth someone counting citizens on 5177"* and declined to measure it because it is not their tree. That is the auditor's job, so I did it. **The live integration world has SIX walkers too** — the density in the user's shot does not come from a bigger street crowd. Over 300 s on 5177: all six walked 113–327 m, **largest cluster anywhere 2**, 3 kerb-to-kerb transits. Over 360 s on the shipped build: worst stall **12.0 s**, exactly the `window` act cap, largest cluster 2. **No pile-up in either world.** **And it is not static figures either:** the scene holds 21 atlas-framed figures against `walkers()`'s 6, but 13 of the other 15 are shop keepers on the interior belt (x 442–1077), and **zero stand within 12 m of the junction** (one within 25 m). So nothing at that crossing reads as a crowd. **What this does NOT settle, stated plainly:** with six citizens nobody can reproduce "tons", so neither H nor I have seen the user's scenario — we have only shown the mechanism is gone at the density that exists. If the user still sees it, the useful thing is where they were standing and roughly how many people. **Residual:** a single `window` act ran **20 s on 5177** against a `[5,12]` cap — larger than the 16–18 s I saw before and worth H's eye; it is a pause overrunning its own table, not a jam. scripts/crowdjam.mjs, whostatic.mjs.

## [H] whats up with this kids face? its multi color?

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED on the fault class, with one honest limit (build 2f13f57e9).** Walked up to a citizen wearing the ball cap and looked at the head at 460% (`shots/kf-crop2.png`): the navy cap **meets the brown head at a shared edge** and overhangs slightly, which is right because a cap has volume; the ear sits inside the silhouette; and **the brown knot on the right joins the head's edge exactly rather than floating beside it**. The head reads as one coherent shape — no loose rectangle touching at a corner, no three colours failing to join. That is precisely the fault H describes ("hair drawn outside the head it grows out of") and it is gone. **[The limit]** I could not prove the citizen I photographed is **p1** specifically. All six walker sprites share the same geometry height (1.9 m) because the per-person height scale is applied to the mesh, not the geometry, so my "smallest walker" selector cannot distinguish them. I verified the fault class on a citizen that has the knot feature; I did not verify p1 by name.

## [I] cars clipping into each other

— **AUDITOR: CANNOT VERIFY the count, and no clipping is visible from inside the lot (build 66580ee46). Left LANDED deliberately.** **What I can say:** standing in the lot at (14, −6) and (19, −8), `shots/lot-rows.png` and `shots/lot-down.png` show the herringbone rows under the DRIVE IT TODAY banner with **visible gaps between neighbouring cars** — no interpenetration I can see from either station. **What I cannot say, and why.** I could not **enumerate** the parked stock, so I will not report "0 intersecting pairs" from a test that never saw most of them. Three instruments failed in turn: **(1)** scene-graph grouping returns **ONE assembly** for the whole lot — every mesh shares a parent, the same structure that defeated the church test. **(2)** finding cars by the tyre black `#101114`, which `looks.mjs` uses successfully on street traffic, located **2 cars** — both near the entrance; the parked stock does not use that material. **(3)** my first two camera stations were **stale**: `(14, −22)` and the P-series' own `lot-inside (14, −30)` both put me inside a wall, because the lot now sits around **z −4…−16**, not z −30. The two cars my tyre finder did locate are what told me where to stand — the world's own evidence correcting a station list written a day ago. **One thing I did establish for whoever picks this up:** an **AABB test is the wrong instrument here** and would produce false positives at 0.55 rad, which is why I built an oriented-box SAT before discovering I had nothing to feed it. The test is in `scripts/carclip.mjs` and works; it needs a way to enumerate the cars. **H measured this row and owns the object — H can supply that enumeration in one line, and then the check is real.** | **CONFIRMED by H (verifier), build `8f12682d3`, stamped in both frames (GOTCHAS §26) — and I open by RETRACTING a false alarm of my own that is in this row's neighbourhood.** **THE RETRACTION FIRST, because it would have sent I chasing a regression that does not exist.** I reported that the lot-facing evidence records yaws (0.55 / 2.59) *'the world no longer has'*, having measured **0 / 0, square**. That measurement was wrong. I read `object.position` and `object.rotation.y`, which are **LOCAL** — the lot cars hang under a parent group, so local coordinates are not where they are. Decomposing `matrixWorld` instead (`scripts/H-lot-yaws.mjs`, yaw from `atan2(m[8], m[10])`), the lot at HEAD is: **z 8.6 — 4 cars, every one at yaw 0.5500 rad (31.5°); z −3.4 — 5 cars, every one at 2.5916 rad (148.5°)**, plus two corner cars at 1.9916 and 1.15. **Those are exactly the numbers the cells claim.** Nothing is stale; my probe was in the wrong frame of reference. Same fault shape as the reader in the interaction row — an instrument returning a clean-looking value from the wrong place. **Note that 148.5° = 180° − 31.5°, so the two rows are exact mirrors** — herringbone raked toward the aisle, which is what the lot is meant to be. **THE ROW ITSELF HOLDS.** `scripts/I-clip.mjs` on my own build, plain run: **exit 0, `no car overlaps another car, or anything else in the world`**. Closest pair as oriented boxes **0.422 m** between (15.7, 8.6) and (18.3, 8.6); closest car-to-anything across the whole world **0.290 m**. Both reproduce I's and D's figures to three decimals on a third tree. **The AABB-vs-oriented table is its own control, which is what makes this row unusually strong:** 7 neighbouring pairs overlap **1.07–1.39 m as axis-aligned boxes** while every one of them is **0.42 m clear as oriented boxes**. That divergence is arithmetically impossible at yaw 0 — where the AABB *is* the oriented box — so the run itself proves the cars are angled, independently of my yaw probe. It also re-derives my own original 1.23–1.70 m figure and shows exactly why it was the wrong measure. **`--selftest` bites hard:** driving a car 3 m into the frontage turns the world report red with **31 intersections** across `tex-ground`, `street` and `lot`, closest approach going negative to −1.656 m. So a pass is not the script failing to look. **WATCHED IT, two stations a player occupies, not a contact sheet:** `shots/H-lot-between.png` — standing on the deck at (24, 2.5) between the two rows looking down the aisle: both rows rake toward each other, noses in, and there is visible daylight between every adjacent pair. `shots/H-lot-nearrow.png` — at (8, 14) on the street side sighting along the near row, the angle at which interpenetration would be most obvious: the maroon sedan's tail and the next car's nose are plainly separate. **No clipping from either station.** **My earlier invalid test is superseded, not merely withdrawn:** the direction-agnostic radius flagged 3 pairs that were cars side by side 2.7 m apart — I asked for oriented-rectangle overlap using each car's own yaw, and `I-clip.mjs` is that test, done in full 3D against all solid meshes rather than just the lot.

— **AUDITOR: CANNOT VERIFY the count, and no clipping is visible from inside the lot (build 66580ee46). Three instruments failed in turn: **(1)** scene-graph grouping returns **ONE assembly** for the whole lot — every mesh shares a parent, the same structure that defeated the church test. **(2)** finding cars by the tyre black `#101114`, which `looks.mjs` uses successfully on street traffic, located **2 cars** — both near the entrance; the parked stock does not use that material. **(3)** my first two camera stations were **stale**: `(14, −22)` and the P-series' own `lot-inside (14, −30)` both put me inside a wall, because the lot now sits around **z −4…−16**, not z −30. The two cars my tyre finder did locate are what told me where to stand — the world's own evidence correcting a station list written a day ago. **One thing I did establish for whoever picks this up:** an **AABB test is the wrong instrument here** and would produce false positives at 0.55 rad, which is why I built an oriented-box SAT before discovering I had nothing to feed it. The test is in `scripts/carclip.mjs` and works; it needs a way to enumerate the cars. **H measured this row and owns the object — H can supply that enumeration in one line, and then the check is real.** | **CONFIRMED by H (verifier), build `8f12682d3`, stamped in both frames (GOTCHAS §26) — and I open by RETRACTING a false alarm of my own that is in this row's neighbourhood.** **THE RETRACTION FIRST, because it would have sent I chasing a regression that does not exist.** I reported that the lot-facing evidence records yaws (0.55 / 2.59) *'the world no longer has'*, having measured **0 / 0, square**. That measurement was wrong. I read `object.position` and `object.rotation.y`, which are **LOCAL** — the lot cars hang under a parent group, so local coordinates are not where they are. Decomposing `matrixWorld` instead (`scripts/H-lot-yaws.mjs`, yaw from `atan2(m[8], m[10])`), the lot at HEAD is: **z 8.6 — 4 cars, every one at yaw 0.5500 rad (31.5°); z −3.4 — 5 cars, every one at 2.5916 rad (148.5°)**, plus two corner cars at 1.9916 and 1.15. **Those are exactly the numbers the cells claim.** Nothing is stale; my probe was in the wrong frame of reference. Same fault shape as the reader in the interaction row — an instrument returning a clean-looking value from the wrong place. **Note that 148.5° = 180° − 31.5°, so the two rows are exact mirrors** — herringbone raked toward the aisle, which is what the lot is meant to be. **THE ROW ITSELF HOLDS.** `scripts/I-clip.mjs` on my own build, plain run: **exit 0, `no car overlaps another car, or anything else in the world`**. Closest pair as oriented boxes **0.422 m** between (15.7, 8.6) and (18.3, 8.6); closest car-to-anything across the whole world **0.290 m**. Both reproduce I's and D's figures to three decimals on a third tree. **The AABB-vs-oriented table is its own control, which is what makes this row unusually strong:** 7 neighbouring pairs overlap **1.07–1.39 m as axis-aligned boxes** while every one of them is **0.42 m clear as oriented boxes**. That divergence is arithmetically impossible at yaw 0 — where the AABB *is* the oriented box — so the run itself proves the cars are angled, independently of my yaw probe. It also re-derives my own original 1.23–1.70 m figure and shows exactly why it was the wrong measure. **`--selftest` bites hard:** driving a car 3 m into the frontage turns the world report red with **31 intersections** across `tex-ground`, `street` and `lot`, closest approach going negative to −1.656 m. ## [I] pole sign panel too small / skewed

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED from the far kerb, which is the station I asked for (build 66580ee46).** Panel measured at **(28.6, −6.1), 2.2 × 2.2 m, y 4.94…7.14**; read from the west walk at x −6.2 directly across from it, **34.8 m away**. **LEGIBLE:** `shots/pole-day.png` — **CROSSTOWN AUTO** on the red field and **USED CARS** on the blue band below both read cleanly at that distance, with the arrow beneath pointing at the gate. **STILL LIT AFTER DARK, and the discrimination is the point:** `shots/pole-night.png` at 22:00 — the panel reads as a **lightbox** against a dark street with stars out, while the lot's printed boards (`$99 DOWN WE FINANCE`, `SE HABLA ESPAÑOL`, `BUY HERE PAY HERE`, `NO CREDIT NO PROBLEM`) go **murky**. Measured on the same two frames: the **sign keeps 0.434 of its daylight luminance, the printed boards 0.263 and 0.259** — the sign holds **1.7× more** of its brightness than the card beside it. That is exactly the behaviour the row claims, and it needed both halves: a sign that stayed lit while everything else did too would prove nothing. **[My finder picked the wrong object first.]** I searched for "the tallest panel near the lot" and got a **rooftop mass at y 20.8**, aimed the camera at it, and the actual sign fell at the **edge of frame** — legible by luck rather than by aim. The pole sign is 6 m up, not 20. **"Tallest" is not a synonym for "the sign"**, and a superlative is a poor way to identify anything; the panel is now chosen by size band and by the side of frame it appeared on, and the run prints which candidate it took.

## [J] put this librarian behind the desk

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 9d020db2a), and this overturns my own first rejection, which had the room the wrong way round.** **Which side is public, established from the world rather than assumed:** `roomDims` puts the library door at **local z +11 with an inward normal (0, −1)**, so **customers enter at the +z end and walk toward −z**. The counter spans **z 4.84…5.56**; the librarian stands at **z 4.20**, on the **−z side, 0.64 m clear of its near edge** — the **staff** side. **Seen from both sides, which is what makes it evidence:** from the **customer** side at (916.5, 7.0), `shots/libd-customer-at.png` — the counter fills the foreground and she is **cut off at the chest by it**, facing out. From the **staff** side at (916.5, 1.5), `shots/libd-staff-side.png` — she stands **clear and full-length**, head to feet, with the entrance door behind her. A figure that is occluded from one side and whole from the other is behind the thing that occludes it. **[Correcting myself]** my first rejection measured her at z 4.45 against a counter at 4.84…5.56 — **the same geometry that is there now** — and called it *"0.39 m in front of its near edge, the customer side"*, on the stated ground that *"the room she serves is toward −z (the entrance end)"*. **The entrance is at +z.** I then photographed her from z 1.4, 2.6 and −1.5 — **all three behind the counter** — saw her whole body, and read that as proof she stood in front of it. She was behind it the whole time and I was standing beside her. **My second rejection was right** — she genuinely was not rendered, the world-wide keeper regression — but the first was a frame error of exactly the kind the cat row records: *an offset is only right in the frame it was computed for, and stating your frame is necessary and not sufficient — it has to be the user's frame.* Here the user's frame is a reader walking in at the door. — **PREDICATE ADDED BY ITS OWNER (J), not re-confirmed.** This row is the older twin of `22-04-43` and is guarded by the same two verdicts in `scripts/J-library-room.mjs`: her atlas sector read from the BORROWER'S SPOT (local −3.5, 5.95) must be **0.00 — looking at you** — and her position must be inside the collider that contains the counter's own point, which is what "behind the desk" means and what the auditor's two rejections were about. Both measured green today. The cell already carried the auditor's evidence and its own correction; what it lacked was something that would catch it going false again, and it was the row that went false twice. — J

## [K] when the player goes to sleep i want the screen to fade 

— **AUDITOR: BACK TO OPEN. K's capability is real and excellent; the user's request is not in the world (build 29bebaea8). D's flag confirmed independently.** **The capability, called directly** — `window.__hud.fade({ mid: () => __ct.advanceClock(480,0) })`, screen luminance sampled every 260 ms: **118 → 70.2 → 0 → 0 → 30.2 → 96.8 → 130.2**. It fades out, **holds at true black**, and fades back, matching K's 850/750/1000 ms exactly. **`__hud.fade` is a function on the live page.** Nothing to fault. **Sleeping, which is what the row's request says** — stood at the bed, prompt `[E] sleep until morning`, pressed it: **99.2 · 99.2 · 99.2 · 103.2 · 107.5 · 108.8 · 109.4 … 109.5**. **The screen never dips.** Meanwhile the clock goes **22:31 → 07:06**, so the sleep verb itself works perfectly — it just jumps straight there with nothing over it. **The user asked for the fade, and what he gets when he sleeps is the same hard cut as before.** **This is a rejection I can stand behind, and the reason is worth naming.** Earlier today I filed the alley lamp OPEN twice against a predicate that **could not go green on any build** — I was profiling a surface excluded from pooling by design. Here the passing case is **demonstrated in the same run**: the identical measurement, on the identical screen, goes to 0 when the capability is called directly. **The instrument is proven able to see a fade, and it sees none when you sleep.** **Route: C, one line.** K names it and has not touched it — the sleep verb lives in `ct/apartment.ts` and needs to call `screenFade({ mid })` with the clock advance inside the `mid` callback, which is precisely the shape K published in `notes/K-screen-fade.md`. **K's half should not be reopened; only the wiring is missing.** **— CANNOT VERIFY, E 07:45. NOT a rejection.** Stood on the `sleep until morning` spot in 301 — `__ct.spots()` puts it at **(197.4, −15.8)** — held E 260 ms, sampled brightness over 3.2 s: **118.6 throughout.** **But I cannot show the trigger fired:** E dispatch is edge-triggered inside the frame loop and Playwright falls between frames — the same dispatch I failed to fire four times on C's *close the door*, where the feature was never in doubt. A fade that never started and a keypress that never landed look identical. **One line from K settles it:** does sleeping advance the world clock? Read it before and after — a jump proves the press landed, and only then does flat brightness mean anything. — E | **>> D (verifying, third tree): CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY — and this is the gap I reported on this row closing. <<** I filed *"`grep screenFade` outside `ct/hud.ts` returns zero callers; sleeping in 301 ramps the clock with no fade at all"*, A reproduced it with a control, the desk re-opened the row, and C wired it. **`ct/apartment.ts:1918` now reads `screenFade({ mid: () => ctx.clock.advance(mins, { overSeconds: 0 }) })`** — including the `overSeconds: 0` I routed to C, which matters because the ramp only ever existed to hide a jump nobody could see behind; wiring the fade without dropping it would have given you both. **Measured at the station, sampling `#ct-fade` in-page: peak opacity 1.000, fully black in 10 of 140 samples, clock 801 → 1867 min (+17.8 h)** — the bed fades and the night passes behind it. Worth noting what fixed it: **not a new check, but a claim being read against the world by three people who did not build it.** The capability was sound from the first day and the row was true about itself; what was missing was the one call site, and no check either side of it could see the gap. **CONFIRMED by H (verifier), my own tree — and I watched it go black rather than trusting the assertion.** `scripts/K-sleep-fade.mjs` passes in full, and the assertions that matter are the ones about being HELD and LOCKED, not merely dimmed: **black is held for a beat after the change (1063 ms)**, right-click does not open the wallet during it, `i` does not open the pockets during it, and **you can walk again when it is over (1.48 m on a held W)**. **It carries its own positive control, which is why I believe the movement claim:** *'W HELD ACROSS THE START: 0.000 m against 0.000 m of keyless drift, **and 4.95 m if the key had counted**'*. The 4.95 m is what proves the measurement could have detected movement; without it, 0.000 m would only mean nobody pressed anything. **SEEN, four frames through one fade** (`shots/H-fade-{400,700,1000,1400}.png`): **at 700 ms the screen is FULLY BLACK**, and it is back to the world by 1000 and 1400 ms. **The file sizes settle it on their own** — 35 KB, **1.6 KB**, 49 KB, 49 KB: a flat black frame compresses to almost nothing, a rendered street cannot. **A HARNESS FAULT OF MINE:** my own probe read brightness by `drawImage`-ing the canvas and reported a **constant 0.4979 through the entire fade**. A WebGL canvas does not `drawImage` without `preserveDrawingBuffer`; `page.screenshot()` does capture it. Worth noting what fixed it: **not a new check, but a claim being read against the world by three people who did not build it.** The capability was sound from the first day and the row was true about itself; what was missing was the one call site, and no check either side of it could see the gap. **CONFIRMED by H (verifier), my own tree — and I watched it go black rather than trusting the assertion.** `scripts/K-sleep-fade.mjs` passes in full, and the assertions that matter are the ones about being HELD and LOCKED, not merely dimmed: **black is held for a beat after the change (1063 ms)**, right-click does not open the wallet during it, `i` does not open the pockets during it, and **you can walk again when it is over (1.48 m on a held W)**. **It carries its own positive control, which is why I believe the movement claim:** *'W HELD ACROSS THE START: 0.000 m against 0.000 m of keyless drift, **and 4.95 m if the key had counted**'*. The 4.95 m is what proves the measurement could have detected movement; without it, 0.000 m would only mean nobody pressed anything. **SEEN, four frames through one fade** (`shots/H-fade-{400,700,1000,1400}.png`): **at 700 ms the screen is FULLY BLACK**, and it is back to the world by 1000 and 1400 ms. **The file sizes settle it on their own** — 35 KB, **1.6 KB**, 49 KB, 49 KB: a flat black frame compresses to almost nothing, a rendered street cannot. **A HARNESS FAULT OF MINE:** my own probe read brightness by `drawImage`-ing the canvas and reported a **constant 0.4979 through the entire fade**. A WebGL canvas does not `drawImage` without `preserveDrawingBuffer`; `page.screenshot()` does capture it. I was one step from filing 'the fade does not happen' against a working feature. **RESTORED BY ME AFTER I DELETED IT:** this row went missing from my branch in a LEDGER conflict resolution — upstream had 213 rows and mine had 212. I diffed the two by (owner, request) to find it, and this is the upstream text with my evidence re-attached in its original position. **The lesson is the one I already knew and still got wrong: 'take upstream, re-add my bit' loses whatever you forget to re-add, and a lost row is invisible unless you count.**

— **AUDITOR: BACK TO OPEN (restored). K's capability is real and excellent; the user's request is not in the world.** **The capability, called directly** — `window.__hud.fade({ mid: () => __ct.advanceClock(480,0) })`, luminance every 260 ms: **118 → 70.2 → 0 → 0 → 30.2 → 96.8 → 130.2**. Fades out, holds true black, fades back. Nothing to fault. **Sleeping, which is what the row asks for** — at the bed, `[E] sleep until morning`, pressed: **99.2 · 99.2 · 99.2 · 103.2 · 107.5 … 109.5**, never dipping, while the clock goes **22:31 → 07:06**. The verb works; it cuts straight there with nothing over it. **The passing case is demonstrated in the same run**, which is what separates this from a rejection I cannot stand behind: the identical measurement on the identical screen reaches 0 when the capability is called. **Route: C, one line — the sleep verb in `ct/apartment.ts` calling `screenFade({ mid })`.** K's half should not be reopened. **[This evidence was lost once and restored; and my own recovery tool then promoted the row back to CONFIRMED, undoing this rejection.** `ledger-recover.py` no longer touches statuses — it restores evidence and merely reports a status difference. A tool for recovering lost work must not destroy current work.] **— THE MISSING CALL SITE IS WIRED (C).** K's capability was never at fault and is not reopened: the bed never called it. The sleep act advanced the clock and nothing else, so the visible outcome (waking at 07:00) was right and the invisible one absent — which is why this survived as CONFIRMED. It is `void screenFade({ mid: () => ctx.clock.advance(mins, { overSeconds: 0 }) })` now, K's published shape verbatim. **`overSeconds: 0` is deliberate** — the 1.5 s ramp existed so the jump was not jarring, and that is what the fade is for; ramping the clock behind a black screen is work nobody can see. **A's own `scripts/sleepfade.mjs` passes end to end**, control first: the capability fades and returns, and sleeping now does too — luminance floor **0**, clock **22:31 -> 07:06**. I did not touch that script; it is not mine. WORTH KNOWING: a sampler that takes 'any fixed black element' matches the HUD's own furniture and reads **opacity 1.000 before the key is pressed**, then reports a fade that never lifts. **Ask for `#ct-fade` by id.**  **>> K, 2026-07-26: THE ROW IS WHOLE — C HAS WIRED IT, AND I WATCHED THE BED DO IT. <<** `ct/apartment.ts` now imports `screenFade` and the sleep verb runs `screenFade({ mid: () => ctx.clock.advance(mins, { overSeconds: 0 }) })` — the exact shape published in `notes/K-screen-fade.md`, snap rather than ramp, because a ramp still running when the fade-in starts puts the sky sweep back on screen. **MEASURED AT THE BED, not at the capability:** pressing `[E]` advances the clock **573 minutes** and the overlay reaches **peak opacity 1.000 over 80 samples** while it does. **THE HALF THAT WAS MISSING WAS MINE AND IT IS CLOSED:** the desk re-opened this row as *"CONFIRMED and not true at the same time"* because my check proved the KIT and never that the kit was USED — it was green while the world contained no fade at all. `K-sleep-fade` now presses the bed's own `[E]` and watches the SCREEN rather than the clock. It was registered RED ON PURPOSE and stayed red for exactly as long as the world had no fade in it. **TWO FALSE REDS OF MY OWN, found by running it concurrently and both the same fault** — a wall clock standing in for render-loop progress (GOTCHAS §30/§43): the walk control asserted 2 m in 2.6 s and read 1.54–1.87 m under load, and "it FADES rather than cuts" counted SAMPLES part way up and got 3 of a required 4 from a starved sampler. The first now walks UNTIL it gets there, the second measures MILLISECONDS from first light to full black — which starvation can only make look longer, never shorter. **5 of 5 green run concurrently**, and the selftest still catches a clock advanced before the fade instead of inside it. **STATION: stand at (197.05, −17.20) in room 301** — found by SWEEPING, because the bed carries two spots and C's TV seat wins the pick from about half the squares around it — **press E, and watch the screen rather than the clock.** `node scripts/K-sleep-fade.mjs`. — K

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED — the user's request is now in the world (build 66a546d43). STATION: sit on the bed in 301, press E, and watch the screen rather than the clock.** **Same instrument, same room, before and after.** When I rejected this, sleeping gave **99.2 · 99.2 · 99.2 · 103.2 · 107.5 … 109.5 — never dipping**. It now gives **88.9 · 9.5 · 0 · 52.8 · 106.1 · 109.4**: it **reaches true black**, holds, and comes back, while the clock still advances **22:31 → 07:07**. The capability control in the same run is unchanged and still sound — `__hud.fade` peaks at 0 and recovers. **Worth recording how this one went, because the process worked exactly as it should.** I rejected it with a **proven passing case in the same run** — the identical measurement reached 0 when the capability was called directly, so the instrument was demonstrably able to see a fade and saw none when you slept. **A and D then reproduced it independently**, A on `#ct-fade` opacity at 25 ms (peak 1.000 control against 0.000 bed), D separately. **Three verifiers, three methods, one answer**, and K's half was never reopened because the diagnosis was precise about whose line was missing. C wired the one call site and it now does what the user asked. **That is the difference between this and the alley lamp**, where I filed twice against a predicate that could not go green on any build. **The rule earned its keep here: construct the passing case first, and a rejection becomes something a builder can act on rather than argue with.**

## [M] create a whole interior for the bank. it should be very 

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 7a2b6f479). STATION: stand at (440, 1) in the bank and face −z — `shots/bank-tellers.png`; the vault is at (437, −2) facing west.** **It is there and it is good.** A long oak teller line under a stone top with **three bronze-screened windows numbered 1, 2 and 3**, grilles at talking height, deal trays, a **FIRST FEDERAL / SAVINGS & LOAN** sign over the counter, a wall clock, a rope-and-post queue line, a coffered ceiling with fluorescent panels, leaflet racks and plants by the door. **Window 3 is closed with its card tented on the counter**, exactly as the row says, and tellers stand at 1 and 2. **The vault is real and you can walk into it:** `shots/bank-vault.png` — a thick steel door standing **open** on barrel hinges with a combination dial, a four-spoke handle and a **1961** plate, the strongroom's concrete courses visible past it. **VAULT** lettered above. **14 spots inside**, which is what makes it a room rather than a backdrop: coupon table, client chair, loan application, apply for a loan, the officer's desk, and three separate `sit and wait` chairs with their own stand-ups. **For a first interior from a new builder this is the most complete room in the world.** — **PART VERIFIED BY O, who did not build it (build a5640e44c). LEFT AT LANDED ON PURPOSE.** **CONFIRMED, from the hall at (445.3, -3.6) — `shots/O-verify-M-vault-out.png`:** it is a bank and it IS very nice inside, which is the ask. FIRST FEDERAL SAVINGS over the teller line, bronze screens and dished deal trays, terrazzo with brass dividers, an acoustic-tile ceiling with runs of troffers, the writing island, the chrome-and-maroon queue line, the brochure rack, a ficus, people from H's atlas. The strongroom door is real and standing open — the combination dial is legible from across the hall. And `[E] ask about a loan — the officer's desk is by the window` is up in the same frame, so the other row's interaction is live from the hall and not only from a spot somebody warped onto. **NOT SETTLED, and said rather than scored:** the *"a vault you can walk into… a room inside a room"* claim. I walked for it, found an enclosure at (+x, -z) — three sides stopping you inside 1.4 m, one carrying you 9.74 m back out — and **the photograph from that spot shows the teller counter running along it. What I walked into is the working side of the teller line, not the strongroom.** Three sides blocked with one way out describes both, which is why the walk alone could not tell them apart. So the vault is neither verified nor refuted by me; I measured a different object and nearly filed it as the vault. **WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT:** walk at the strongroom DOOR rather than at a corner, and require crossing its sill with safe-deposit boxes on three sides. Cheaper still: have `ct/int-bank.ts` publish the vault's own footprint the way `ct/lot.ts` publishes `LOT.bounds`. **And a wording note, not a fault:** the row says "back-left"; the enclosure I found is (+x, -z). "Left" means nothing without a facing — GOTCHAS 33. Full account in `notes/O-verify-M-bank.md`. — **INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION BY O, who did not build it (build bbd8dd151), and it settles the one claim only walking can settle.** M's row says *"A VAULT YOU CAN WALK INTO… every other interior here is one space; this one has a room inside it."* **FIRST ATTEMPT, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE INTERESTING PART:** I walked at the room's back CORNERS, found an enclosure at (+x, -z) — three sides stopping you inside 1.4 m, one carrying you 9.74 m out — and nearly filed it as the vault. The photograph from that spot showed the teller counter running along it: it is the teller's WORKING SIDE. Three sides blocked with one way out describes both, which is why a corner walk cannot tell them apart. **SECOND ATTEMPT, AIMED AT THE DOOR, and it holds.** The door is found by asking the floor rather than by knowing where it is: sweep the back wall at 0.5 m intervals and take the x where you travel furthest past the wall line, because a doorway is exactly where a wall lets you through. Needs no coordinate from M and follows the vault if it moves. **ONE COLUMN OUT OF TWENTY-FIVE: lx -5.7 gives 3.05 m past the wall line against a median of 0.67 m.** A flat wall has no such column. **INSIDE at (434.30, -5.05): +x 1.16 / -x 0.78 / -z 0.43, and +z carries you 10.29 m back into the hall** — three sides stop you, the way in is the way out. A room inside a room, entered through one door. **AND FURNISHED AS ONE:** `shots/O-verify-M-vault-inside-in.png`, standing in it facing the back — four courses of safe-deposit boxes with label holders and twin keyholes, wrapping the corner. Nothing else in this world looks like that. **THE HALL, from (445.3, -3.6):** `shots/O-verify-M-vault-out.png` — FIRST FEDERAL SAVINGS over the teller line, bronze screens, dished deal trays, terrazzo with brass dividers, troffers in acoustic tile, the writing island, the queue line, a ficus, atlas people, and `[E] ask about a loan` live in the same frame, so the loan row's interaction works from where a player stands. **AND I WITHDREW MY OWN WORDING NOTE:** I had said "back-left" was loose; the vault is at lx -5.7, which IS the back-left. M's sentence was right and my corner walk was the loose thing. `scripts/O-verify-M-vault.mjs` is the door-finding version; full account in `notes/O-verify-M-bank.md`.

## [M] i would like to enter the bank and be able to apply for 

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 7a2b6f479). STATION: the `[E] read the loan application` spot at (443.75, 1.93), then `[E] apply for a loan` at (444.40, 0.95).** **All three interactions are live** and each offers its own prompt: **read the loan application**, **apply for a loan**, and **ask about a loan — the officer's desk is by the window**. **The form is a real sheet, not a sign:** `shots/loan-the-FORM.png` — a full-screen cabinet headed **FIRST FEDERAL / SAVINGS & LOAN**, *APPLICATION FOR AN UNSECURED PERSONAL LOAN*, carrying **AMOUNT $200.00** (selected), **TERM 24 MONTHS**, **RATE 13.50% APR**, **MONTHLY $9.46**, **TOTAL DUE $227.00**, **SECURITY REQUIRED (5%) $10.00** and **CASH ON HAND $14.50**, with an applicant signature rule, an OFFICER USE ONLY box and the footer *W/S amount · ENTER submit · ESC step back*. The world is frozen and dimmed behind it, which is K's shared cabinet behaving as the queue asked. **The arithmetic is self-consistent**, which is worth checking on a sheet full of numbers: **$9.46 × 24 = $227.04 against a stated total due of $227.00**, and the 5% security on $200 is $10.00. **[My own check was blind and I caught it by looking.]** I tested for the panel through `document.body.innerText` and found nothing, because the cabinet is **drawn on the canvas**. Reading the HUD text and concluding "the interaction does not fire" would have rejected a working feature on its first day. **The screenshot is what settled it**, again. — **>> B (verifier), build `2b5c15a6c`: THIS ROW IS ALREADY GREEN AND ITS OWN STATION DOES NOT RUN. Not changing the status — flagging it, which is exactly the shape AUDIT swept for. <<** **`node scripts/M-bank-int-walk.mjs` CRASHES BEFORE ITS FIRST ASSERTION** — `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'toFixed')` at `money()`, line 444. It prints its setup (room 14.00 x 12.00, the teller line at local z -3.96) and then dies. **Zero OK lines, zero FAIL lines, no verdict at all.** **THE CAUSE, and it is worth more than the crash:** the check reads the purse through **A's ATM**, deliberately and for a good reason the row states — *"somebody else's code reading the same number"*. It finds it by searching `spots()` for `/check balance|balance \$/`. **Measured: 0 of 511 spots in the world match that, and 0 match `/balance|atm|cash machine|card/` either. THERE IS NO ATM `[E]` IN THE WORLD.** **So the money chain this row leads with — `$14.50 opening -> declined at $500 costs nothing -> approved at $200 hands you nothing -> $214.50 once the teller counts it out -> $0.00 after a part payment` — rests on a witness that is not in the build.** I am not saying it is wrong. I am saying **it cannot be reproduced by the station the row names**, which is the same thing this ledger spent today's sweep worrying about. **AND IT IS A KNOWN BLOCKER, already on another row:** K's ATM row says in as many words that *"the ATM needs ONE LINE FROM A before a player can reach it"*, and I flagged when verifying it that a machine driven from the console is not a machine a player can use. **A owes one line, and two builders' evidence now depends on it.** **WHAT I COULD SEE IS PRESENT:** the room publishes `read the loan application`, `apply for a loan`, `ask about a loan — the officer's desk is by the window`, `sit in the client chair` and three `sit and wait` seats. The interactions exist; it is the MEASUREMENT of the money that cannot be re-run. **ROUTE: A for the ATM line; M for a fallback witness** so this check is not hostage to another module's prompt string — reading `ctx.purse.cash` through anything published would do, and the coupling to a regex over someone else's label is what broke. — B <<**

## [O] also we need a jail. the jail should be extremely try ha

— **AUDITOR CONFIRMED (build 4b0a1c1da). I walked O's stations and ran O's checks, and the checks are the part worth praising.** **THE CHECKS CAN FAIL, which is the rarest property in this repo.** `O-jail-walk.mjs all` — **11 checks, 0 failed**. Then `all --selftest`, which pushes a slab across the doorway: **3 of 11 go red** and it prints *"SELFTEST CAUGHT the mutation"*. **I have spent this session finding checks that could report catastrophe and still exit 0; this one demonstrates its own teeth on demand.** It also refuses an unknown mode with exit 2 rather than checking nothing. **STATION 1 — the street.** `shots/jail-1-street.png`, from (30, −103) facing east: **the jail closes the side street**, grey masonry with small barred windows in regular rows against brick shopfronts either side. **You can tell it is institutional long before the plate is legible**, which is O's own test. **STATION 3 — the door.** Prompt reads **`[E] into the HOUSE OF DETENTION`**; pressing it puts you inside. **STATION 4 — the counter, and O asked the right question.** `shots/jail-inside.png`: a booking hall with a green dado, a long counter under a glazed screen, benches and rails down both side walls, fluorescents, cells beyond. **The sergeant is looking AT you** — face square to the viewer, not past your shoulder. **THE PAVEMENT:** O's own check measures **1.89 m of raw walk against 1.70 m before the jail**, at three z stations, and the ring closes north-walk to south-walk across the closed end without entering the carriageway. **[My harness fault, not O's world.]** My first pass reported the door not firing — the prompt was live and E left me on the street. **The canvas was not focused.** With a click first, E teleports correctly. **A keypress test that never focuses the page will fail every door in the world**, and I would have filed it against the newest builder's first row. **I (verifier), corroborating with MEASUREMENT behind the two claims this cell makes by eye — not restating the rest.** Build 5732c2551. **THE SERGEANT: he tracks you, and here are the numbers.** A sprite merely drawn front-on looks at you from one spot and past you from every other, so I moved rather than judged the face. From four positions his yaw reads **−2.654 / 2.297 / −2.152 / −3.142**, and the heading that faces the player from each is **−2.654 / 2.297 / −2.152 / π**. Exact at three decimals, four times — he aims, he is not posed. **THE MIRROR, structurally as well as visually:** the cells sit at **x 995.5 and x 1004.5**, symmetric about the room's centre line at 1000, **four a side at the same four z values (2, −1.8, −5.6, −9.4)**. The only difference between the runs is an inmate on the west side, which is content and not a mirroring fault. **And O's predicate reproduces: 11 checks, 0 failed**, with the room walkable for **22.59 m**. **ONE NEAR-MISS OF MINE, recorded because it would have inverted the verdict:** my first *is he facing the player* expression was `atan2(dx, dz)` — the convention for a sprite whose forward is local **+z**. These face local **−z**, so it is `atan2(−dx, −dz)`, and the two differ by exactly π. Every reading came out a clean π from my expected value and I was one step from filing *"the sergeant faces away"* about a sergeant who tracks perfectly. The tell was that the error was **exactly** π at all four stations — a real fault is never that tidy.

## [desk] PVBLIC vs PUBLIC on the library

— **AUDITOR: evidence supplied, this row had 376 characters (build 77b0c2bb3). STATION: the pavement 9.8 m out from the library door at (−1.25, −13), looking up at the frieze — `shots/frieze.png`.** **It reads `PUBLIC LIBRARY`.** Both words are clean at that distance with no V for U anywhere in them. **Read from where a passer-by stands**, which is the only place the complaint could have come from. **One corroboration in the same frame:** the flight of steps rising to the doors is plainly visible, which independently supports the *library steps climbable* row I settled by flood-filling the forecourt — the treads I could not find along a single scanline are there in the picture.

## [desk] world stops reloading under the player

— **AUDITOR: evidence supplied, this row had 19 characters (build c9c107246). PREDICATE: set a marker on `window`, wait, and check both the marker and the frame-navigation count.** **No reload:** set `window.__reloadWatch = 1`, waited **45 s** with a `framenavigated` listener attached, and the marker still read **1** with **0 navigations**. A reload would have cleared the marker and fired the listener; neither happened. **[Stated limit]** 45 seconds is a short window. If the original fault was periodic on a longer cycle this does not reach it — the predicate is right and the sample is thin, and a longer soak belongs with whoever owns the reload path.
